# Untangle decision log (temporary)

Started 2026-11-11 from the `/untangle-codebase` skill. **Delete this file when the last slice is done** (CLAUDE.md: executed plan files are deleted).
Rules for this work: never change scoring or ranking meaning; one fix per commit; gates after every commit
(`npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run knip`, nearby tests); full gates at the end of each slice.

Baseline on the clean tree (2026-11-11): typecheck, lint, knip, build and size pass.
`npm run test:coverage`: 875 test files, 8,012 tests, all pass, coverage floors hold.
Size: 1,393 source files, ~144k lines. Main bundle 78.24 kB gzip; all JS 1.27 MB gzip (limit 1.3 MB).

## How we know code is dead

`npm run knip` treats every test file as an entry point, so code only a test imports looks "used".
Two independent checks agree on 44 production files that nothing in production imports:

1. an import-graph script (roots: `src/main.tsx`, `src/index.css`, `src/lib/mcp/**`), and
2. `knip --production` with a temporary config (entries `index.html`, `src/main.tsx`, `src/lib/mcp/**`).

## Slice 1: remove dead files (this slice)

Problems:

- 12 files under `src/components/playoffs/` (`match-card/*`, `animation/BracketAnimationUtils.tsx`) hang off the dead `PlayoffMatchCard`. The live card is `src/components/schedule/match-card/`.
- 7 display components nobody renders: `schedule/ScoreSection`, `stats/{AnimatedRankNumber,CompactStandings,StatsSummaryCards}`, `stats/rank/TeamLogo`, `teams/TeamStats`, `timeslots/WeekTimeslotDisplay`, plus `styles/interactionUtils.ts`.
- 3 old team services nobody calls: `TeamAnalysisService` (coach notes; the `team_analysis` table STAYS), `TeamCalculationService`, `TeamHiddenService`.
- 5 old playoff hooks replaced by `usePlayoffViewModel.compat.ts`: `usePlayoffActions`, `usePlayoffBracketData`, `usePlayoffMatches`, `usePlayoffState`, `usePlayoffViewModel`; plus `matches/useMatchInteractions`, `matches/utils/matchResultUtils`, `useWeekTimeslotsByTeam`.
- Old utils: `autoScheduleUtils`, `teamStatsUtils/{fetchTeamData,parseTeamStats}`, `teamDetailsUtils/{headToHeadUtils,matchUtils}`, `colors/{championshipColors,sweepRateColors}`, `powerScore/getTrendingTeams`, `brackets/mappers/teamMapper`, `brackets/validators/bracketDataValidator`, `hooks/useHistoricalPowerScores`.

Fix:

- Delete the files and the tests that only test them. **Edit, do not delete**, test files that also guard live code: `utils/colors/__tests__/colorModules.test.ts`, `utils/teamDetailsUtils/__tests__/teamDetailsUtils.test.ts`, `hooks/playoffs/__tests__/uncoveredPlayoffHooks.test.tsx`.
- Remove `vi.mock` lines and comments that point at deleted files.
- Fix docs that name deleted files (`src/utils/powerScore/README.md`, `src/docs/TABLE_PATTERNS.md`, `TESTING.md`).
- After deleting, run `npm run knip`. Remove only the exports and types it now flags (they lost their last user).

Deliberately NOT in Slice 1 (checked by the scoring and skeptic reviews):

- ~60 other exports that only tests use (`utils/autoSchedule/*`, `validation.ts`, `timeUtils.ts`, ...). Deleting one exposes the next; later.
- `usePreviousRankings`: NOT dead. It feeds the trend arrows (database first, old browser key as fallback).
- `utils/rankingUtils/{index,sortAndUpdateRankings}.ts`: dead wrappers, but `rankingUtils/__tests__/index.test.ts` imports through the barrel and is the only test of the non-power-score column sorts. Repoint that import first (Slice 2/5). **`sortAndUpdateRankings.ts` DONE in dead-code PR D2** (see "Dead code D2 result").
- `GameRules.hardCap`: `winnerDetection.test.ts:8` is the only test of the confirmed "no hard cap" rule. **DONE in dead-code PR D2** (a "no hard cap" pin test was added first, then `hardCap` was removed).
- `matchesCreated` listener: touches `useScoreEntryData` and 5 mass-score tests for a tiny gain. **DONE in dead-code PR D1** (see "Dead code D1 result").
- (Removed in D3b; the rule is pinned on the live path.) `BracketWriteService.updatePlayoffMatchResult`: its test (`BracketWriteService.test.ts:118`) is the only test that every playoff write carries `updated_at`. Before this export is ever removed, add `updated_at: expect.any(String)` to the `objectContaining` in `uncoveredPlayoffHooks.test.tsx` (~line 238).
- `AMBIGUOUS_SCORES`, `clearMatchHistoryCache`, `UPSET_THRESHOLD` (definition), `updateTeamSeed`, `getDivisionGradientClass` in `styles/design-system/divisions.ts`: live or used as test seams.
- `TeamCalculationService.test.ts:53` is the only test of the `sos || 0.5` fallback that lives in `hooks/useTeamRankings.ts:88` (display only). Consider porting that one case later.

No scoring file changed. No database or migration change.

## Slice 2 PR T result (type homes, built 2026-11-11, 8 commits on the PR A branch)

Types are erased at build time, so nothing the league sees can change. Moved type text is character-identical (only the `export` keyword was added to two interfaces in T6). No alias re-exports were left behind: each old place stopped exporting and every importer was repointed in the same commit.

- **T1:** deleted `types/phase3.ts` (six aliases such as `Phase3X = X`, two users) and its line in the `types/index.ts` barrel. `usePlayoffEditMatch.ts` now imports from `services/brackets/read/types`; `TeamSeedService.ts` from `types/seeding`.
- **T2:** `SimpleBracketData` moved from `useBracketData.ts` to `transformBracketData.ts` (it is that function's return type). The `useBracketData` / `transformBracketData` cycle is gone. `BracketLoadingStep` and `BracketLoadingProgress` stay in `useBracketData.ts`.
- **T3:** `TrendBasis` moved from `services/rankings/weeklyTrendsForWeek.ts` to `types/powerScoreSnapshot.ts`. A stale comment in `types/recapEdition.ts` was fixed.
- **T4:** `LetterGrade` moved from `utils/reportCardUtils.ts` to new `types/reportCard.ts`. Grade cutoffs, `GRADE_GPA`, `GRADE_WEIGHTS`, `GradeCategory` and `TeamGrades` stay in `reportCardUtils.ts`. Five importers were repointed (hook, recap service, `types/recapEdition.ts`, two tests).
- **T5:** `WeeklyUpset` and `TeamStreakInfo` moved from `services/weeklyRecap/types.ts` to new `types/weeklyRecap.ts`. `RecapMode`, `WeeklyRecapData` and `MIN_STREAK_COUNT` stay. The two names left the `WeeklyRecapService.ts` re-export; three home components, `upsets.ts`, `streaks.ts` and `types/recapEdition.ts` were repointed.
- **T6:** `OpponentRecord`, `TeamOpponentHistory` and `SeasonOpponentData` moved from `MatchHistoryService.ts` to new `types/seasonOpponentHistory.ts` (not `opponentHistory.ts`: `OpponentHistory` already exists in `types/headToHead.ts`). The re-exports in `MatchReadService.ts` and `useSeasonOpponentHistory.ts` were deleted. `utils/exportMatchupsToExcel.ts` no longer imports from a hook.
- **T7:** `ProfileService.updateProfile` now takes its own small input type (`username`, optional `fullName`) instead of `ProfileFormData` from a component. `ProfileFormData` stays `z.infer` in `components/profile/profileSchema.ts`, like the other forms; `ProfileForm.tsx` still passes its data and TypeScript still checks the shape there.
- **Layer edges:** imports from `src/types/**` up into services, utils or components went from 6 to 2. Also gone: service -> component (`ProfileService`), util -> hook (`exportMatchupsToExcel`) and the hook cycle in T2.
- **Stays on purpose (goes to PR B):** `types/index.ts:115` and `types/playoff.ts:5` (both import `utils/playoffs/playoffTypes`), and the `autoSchedule.ts` / `index.ts` `Team` cycle. `types/badges.ts:3` imports generated Supabase types, which is fine.
- **New type files are not in the `types/index.ts` barrel.** Import them by direct path, like `recapEdition.ts` and `seeding.ts`.
- **Checks so far:** after each commit, typecheck, lint on the changed files, knip and the nearby tests passed. The full suite, coverage floors, build comparison and import-graph proof are the QA step.

(history: superseded 2026-11-16: the career fetch services no longer turn 0 into 0.85; the team weight keeps a stored 0, and a missing weight is null)

## Slice 3 PR 3A result (built 2026-11-12, 1 config commit + 1 docs commit)

3A makes the "no Supabase client in hooks" lint rule real and writes it down. Config and docs only: **no file under `src/` changed**, so nothing a player or admin sees can change. No database or migration change.

- **The hole (found by reading `eslint.config.js`):** the ban on the Supabase client was switched off for every file in `src/hooks/**`. A hook that called `supabase.from(...)` passed lint. Nothing was broken: the 12 production hooks that import the client only call `.channel()` or `.removeChannel()` (checked by grep).
- **3A-1, allow-list by file (commit 228de1b):** the folder glob `src/hooks/**/*.{ts,tsx}` is gone. The ban is off for 12 named hook files only: `useBracketsManagerRealtime`, `useContactRequests`, `useLiveMatchRealtime`, `useMatchComments`, `useMatchReactions`, `useMessageReactions`, `useMessageRealtime`, `useNotificationsRealtime`, `subscribeWithRetry`, `useSupportTickets`, `useBracketCompletion`, `useOpsHealth` (full paths in `eslint.config.js`). A new realtime hook must be added to that list: lint fails at once if it is not. The other exceptions are unchanged (`src/services/**`, `src/utils/imageUpload.ts`, `src/lib/mcp/**`, `src/pages/OAuthConsent.tsx`, test files).
- **3A-1, two side doors closed:** (1) the ban used `paths`, which matched only the exact string `@/integrations/supabase/client`; a relative import (`../integrations/supabase/client`) passed. It now uses `patterns` with `**/integrations/supabase/client`, same `importNames: ['supabase']` (so `src/main.tsx`, which imports `isSupabaseConfigured`, still passes) and the same message. (2) Two `no-restricted-syntax` selectors now block dynamic `import('...supabase/client')` and `require('...supabase/client')`. No such call existed anywhere.
- **3A-1, comments fixed:** the two comments that described the exception list wrongly now say the hooks are listed by file.
- **3A-2, docs:** `CLAUDE.md` Architecture Rules lists the real exceptions; `ARCHITECTURE.md` has a new "Layer rules" section (direction, what lint enforces today versus convention, the exception list, `src/lib/mcp/**` exempt). This log was updated.
- **Probe results:** the table below. Throwaway worktrees, probe files named `zzProbe*` (deleted with the worktrees), baseline = 1d6f4f4.

| Probe                                                                                                                                                                | Baseline                                           | After 3A-1                   |

| (b) unlisted hook, relative import                                                                                                                                   | PASS (hole)                                        | FAIL, once                   |
| (c) new file in `src/hooks/realtime/`                                                                                                                                | PASS (hole)                                        | FAIL                         |

## Slice 3 PR 3B result (built 2026-11-12, 1 config commit + 1 docs commit)

3B makes lint block NEW wrong-way static imports. Config and docs only: **no file under `src/` changed**, so nothing a player or admin sees can change. No package, database or migration change.

- **The rule:** core `no-restricted-imports` with `patterns` and an anchored `regex` in `eslint.config.js`. Zones: `src/types`, `src/utils`, `src/services`, `src/hooks`. `types` and `utils` may not import services, hooks, components or pages; services may not import hooks, components or pages; hooks may not import components or pages. It covers `import`, `import type`, `export ... from` and `export *` on `@/...` and `../...` paths. Messages are plain words and point to `ARCHITECTURE.md` → Layer rules. Tests are exempt (as with the Supabase ban); exactly one test file crosses layers today (`src/hooks/scheduling/__tests__/useScrollToLinkedMatch.test.tsx:6`, QA counted with tests not exempt).
- **Config shape:** the Supabase pattern, the 12 realtime hook paths and the layer table are constants at the top of the file. A later flat-config block REPLACES the earlier `no-restricted-imports` options (no merge), so the blocks run in this order: zone blocks (types, utils, hooks get the Supabase ban plus the layer ban; services get the layer ban only, they own the client) -> Supabase-exempt files that still sit in a zone (the 12 hooks, `imageUpload.ts`: layer ban only) -> 3 per-file exception blocks (4 files) (each lists the Supabase pattern again) -> the "off" block LAST, now only `src/lib/mcp/**`, `OAuthConsent.tsx` and tests. The old single "off" block would have switched the new ban off for services and the 12 hooks.
- **Before the exceptions the rule flagged exactly 4 imports** (lint of `src/` in a scratch copy with the 4 exception blocks removed): `src/utils/adminTabs.ts:1` -> `@/components/admin/dashboard/adminSections`; `src/utils/autoSchedule/matchHistoryService.ts:1` and `src/utils/autoSchedule/compatibilityUtils.ts:1` -> `@/services/matches/MatchReadService`; `src/hooks/useToast.ts:3` -> `@/components/ui/toast` (types).
- **The 4 exceptions** each allow exactly one module and still ban every other layer and the Supabase client. Removal: the three util files go with the design PR for the leftover wrong-way imports (`adminTabs` with the admin cycle; the two autoSchedule files by injecting the service from hooks); the `useToast` block stays as the documented type-only exception. A comment above each block names its cleanup. A stale block does not warn, so delete it by hand when its cleanup lands. (The `adminTabs` block was deleted in design PR D1; 2 blocks remain, covering 3 files.)
- **Why the exceptions are in the config, not inline `eslint-disable` comments:** the factory integrity tool flags every added `eslint-disable` (only Doug can waive that), and the config keeps `src/` untouched.
- **Not covered (stays a convention):** dynamic `import()` (the 14 page loaders in `src/utils/routePrefetch.ts`; they go away with the design PR), `require()`, type-position `import('x').T`, a hop through `@/contexts` or `@/lib`, and `./../x` paths. A `.ts` suffix: the layer ban still catches `@/services/X.ts`, an exception module written with `.ts` is flagged (loud), and the Supabase pattern still misses `.../supabase/client.ts` (a 3A limit, left as it was on purpose). `contexts`, `lib`, `config`, `constants`, `styles`, `icons` and `integrations` are not constrained (0 hits today; decide in Slice 4). `types` -> `utils` is not banned (the layer rules do not say so). (routePrefetch: done in design PR D2)
- **Probes** (ESLint Node API on the real paths, throwaway worktrees at 651d614 with and without the new config): 158 probes, 0 unexpected. 65 layer violations pass at baseline (the hole) and fail now: util, service, hook and type files; `@/`, `../` and `../../` paths; `import type`, `export ... from`, `export *`; 3 of the 12 realtime hooks and `imageUpload.ts` (they still obey the layer ban); each exception file for any other layer and for the lookalikes (`...adminSectionsExtra`, `...adminSections/sub`, `...MatchReadServiceExtra`, `components/ui/toaster`). 24 Supabase probes fail before and after (alias, relative, dynamic `import()` and `require()` in a non-listed hook, util, type, component, page and in each of the 4 exception files: the exception blocks keep the 3A ban). 69 pass before and after: hook -> service, hook -> hook, hook -> context, component -> service, util -> util, util -> types, service -> util; `./hooks/x`; `@/utils/hooks/x`; npm paths with `/components/`; the regex traps `@x/hooks`, `ab/services/x`, `@/servicesFoo`; the Supabase client in services and the 12 listed hooks; `src/main.tsx`; tests, `src/lib/mcp/**`, `OAuthConsent.tsx`; each exception file's one allowed module; the two known gaps `./../services/x` and `@/services.ts`.
- **Resolved config** (`calculateConfigForFile`): services, `imageUpload.ts` and the 12 hooks get the layer pattern only; the 4 exception files and every other hook, util and type file get Supabase plus layer; components and pages get Supabase only; tests, `src/lib/mcp/**` and `OAuthConsent.tsx` resolve to off.
- **Checks:** whole-repo `npm run lint` exit 0 with 0 messages (101 s in the real tree, 87 s in a scratch copy with the new config, 95 s at baseline in the same session; 3A measured 85.4 s: no measurable change). Typecheck and knip exit 0.

3C makes components, hooks and tests use the public index of the `BracketAdmin` folder instead of its internal files. Types plus one pure function: **no logic changed**, nothing a player or admin sees can change. No package, database or migration change.

- **What changed (code commit de5c77a):** `src/services/brackets/manager/services/BracketAdmin/index.ts` kept its `BracketAdminService` class and gained 11 type names (`EditTeamsOptions`, `EditTeamsPreview`, `EditMatchTeamsParams`, `TeamChoice`, `RearrangeBoard`, `RearrangePlanResult`, `RearrangeSlotView`, `SlotAssignment`, `SnapshotSlot`, `LoserSwapSlot`, `SwapLoserSlotsParams`) plus the value `simulateRearrange` (a pure function), 12 new names in all. The 3A plan list said "6 type imports"; the real count was 11 type names. 16 outside files now import from `@/services/brackets/manager/services/BracketAdmin`: 5 components (`RearrangeBracketDialog` is the only value import; `SwapLoserSlotsDialog`; `EditMatchParticipantsDialog`, 3 imports merged into 1; `rearrange/RearrangeBoard`, alias kept; `rearrange/dropLogic`), 4 hooks (`useEditTeams`, `usePlayoffEditMatchParticipants`, `usePlayoffLoserSwap`, `usePlayoffRearrange`) and 7 tests (`dropLogic.test.ts`, the three dialog tests, and `tests/bracketLoserRearrange.integration.test.ts`, `tests/bracketEditTeamsRace.test.ts`, `tests/bracketEditTeams.integration.test.ts`). `eslint --fix` left the lines as written.

The bundle is unchanged because `Playoffs.tsx` already reaches the `BracketAdmin` index through `BracketManagerService`, and `simulate.ts` is pure.

Typecheck, knip, `eslint` on the 17 files and `prettier --check`: exit 0. `grep -rnE "BracketAdmin/(rearrange|editTeams|swap|queries|shapes)" src tests e2e` now finds only the 5 imports in the 3 sibling services.

## Design PR D1 result (built 2026-11-12; 4 commits: code, docs, QA follow-up test, docs fixes)

D1 removes the last util -> component import in `src/utils/adminTabs.ts` and, with it, the last import loop. The admin dashboard redirect behaves the same in every case. No package, database or migration change.

- **What changed (code commit ca646aa):**
  - `readRememberedAdminSection` in `src/utils/adminTabs.ts` now returns the stored text, or `null` for a first visit or when `sessionStorage` is refused. It no longer imports `adminSections`.
  - `src/pages/AdminDashboard.tsx` does the check: for a bare `/admin` it reads the remembered text and goes to it only if `isAdminSectionId(...)` says it names a section, else to `DEFAULT_ADMIN_SECTION` (`timeslots`). `isAdminSectionId` already accepts `string | null | undefined`, so no extra null guard was needed.
  - The `adminTabs` exception block and its comment are deleted from `eslint.config.js`. The `matchHistoryService` / `compatibilityUtils` block and the `useToast` block stay.
  - A comment in `src/utils/adminPath.ts` that said `adminTabs` "reaches" `adminSections` now says it imported it.
- **Counts:** wrong-way import edges 18 -> 17 (14 dynamic `routePrefetch` loaders, the 2 scheduler files, 1 `useToast` type import). The 9-file loop (`adminSections` lazy-loads four tab components; those import `adminTabs`; `adminTabs` imported `adminSections`) is gone, because `adminTabs.ts:1` was the only edge from the loop back into `adminSections`. QA re-verifies the loop count (1 -> 0) with the import-graph script. (routePrefetch: done in design PR D2)
- **Behaviour of a bare `/admin`, before and after (identical):**

| Stored `adminActiveTab`         | Before             | After                                       |
| ------------------------------- | ------------------ | ------------------------------------------- |
| a valid id (e.g. `divisions`)   | `/admin/divisions` | same                                        |
| an old id that no longer exists | `/admin/timeslots` | same (the page check fails, so the default) |
| empty text                      | `/admin/timeslots` | same                                        |
| nothing stored                  | `/admin/timeslots` | same                                        |
| `sessionStorage` throws         | `/admin/timeslots` | same                                        |

- **Tests:** 2 edited, 1 added; none removed, skipped or weakened. In `src/utils/__tests__/adminTabs.test.ts` two cases expected the util to answer `'timeslots'`; they now expect `null` (nothing stored, storage refused) and the raw stale id returned unchanged, with the same number of `expect`s. In `src/pages/__tests__/AdminDashboard.test.tsx` one case is added: when `Storage.prototype.getItem` throws, `/admin` ends at `/admin/timeslots`. The existing valid / invalid / missing cases there pass unchanged and now cover the fallback end to end. Run in `adminTabs.test.ts`, `AdminDashboard.test.tsx`, `src/components/admin` and `src/pages`: 148 files, 1,189 tests before; 148 files, 1,190 tests after.
- **QA follow-up:** QA found the page guard unpinned for an empty or malformed stored value (swapping it for `remembered ?? DEFAULT_ADMIN_SECTION` left every test green), so D1 now has 3 more tests for them (empty text, `divisions?x=1`, `Divisions`; the first two fail under the weaker guard), and QA's behaviour matrix of 45 cases showed 0 differences between baseline and the new code.
- **Checks (implementer):** typecheck, knip, scoped eslint and whole-repo `npm run lint` exit 0 (0 messages; the deleted exception block changed nothing). `grep -rn adminSections src/utils` finds only the one comment in `adminPath.ts`.
- **Rejected alternatives:**
  - A leaf module for the section ids: it needs a fourth copy of the 22-id list beside `ADMIN_SECTIONS`, `ADMIN_SECTION_GROUPS` and `adminSectionGuide`, which can drift.
  - Moving `adminTabs.ts` into `components/`: it does not break the loop (a helper checked).
- **Remaining Slice 2/3 list is now:** D2 (`routePrefetch`), D3 (scheduler history), 3D-3J (optional). `hooks/useToast.ts` stays as the documented type-only exception. (routePrefetch: done in design PR D2)

## Design for D2 and D3 (from the 2026-11-12 recon; plans still to be approved)

Three read-only helpers designed the fix for each cluster. They are separate PRs because the risk differs a lot. D2 and D3 each need their own plan and Doug's approval before any code.

| PR  | Cluster                                                                                                                                                                                              | Edges                                                                  | Risk                                                                                                                                    |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| D1  | `utils/adminTabs.ts` (DONE, see above)                                                                                                                                                               | 18 -> 17, loop 1 -> 0                                                  | Low                                                                                                                                     |
| D2  | `utils/routePrefetch.ts` (DONE, see "Design PR D2 result" below): 14 dynamic page imports. `App.tsx` registers its page loaders (it already lazy-loads all 14).                                      | 17 -> 3                                                                | Low-Med (touches `App.tsx`, the root; main chunk shrinks about 3.9 KB; needs browser tests)                                             |
| D3  | The auto-scheduler's history helpers (DONE, see "Design PR D3 result" below): move `matchHistoryService` into `services/matches/` and pass the functions in from the hooks; delete one dead function | 3 -> 1 (only the `useToast` type import stays, a documented exception) | Higher: league-night path (auto-schedule); deletes dead code and 3 tests (the factory integrity tool will flag it; only Doug can waive) |

- **D2 (`routePrefetch`, DONE, see "Design PR D2 result" below):** 2 exports (`prefetchRoute`, `preloadCoreRoutes`); 13 live keys; the `teamDetails` loader (line 10) is never used; `preloadCoreRoutes()` runs once in an effect after `AppLayout` commits; `prefetchRoute` runs on hover/focus/touch of nav links (`NavLinks.tsx`, `NavItem.tsx`); `App.tsx` has 14 matching `lazy(() => import('./pages/X'))` lines; built output shows each page is ONE chunk with two `import()` sites in the main entry; plan: `App.tsx` defines one loader per page, uses it for `lazy(...)` AND passes the map to `registerRoutePrefetchers(...)`; tests to change: `routePrefetch.test.ts` (rewrite), `admin-gating.test.tsx` (add one mock key); keep `App.tsx` free of the text `path="..."` in comments (two tests read it as text); `e2e/offline.spec.ts` must stay true.
- **D3 (scheduler history, DONE, see "Design PR D3 result" below):** (1) the database half of `compatibilityUtils.ts` (`haveTeamsPlayed`, `checkTeamsPlayedHistory`) is dead in production; (2) `TeamPairingConfig.haveTeamsPlayedFn` is required but never called; (3) `generateDualBlockPairings` has no production caller; (4) `generatePairings.ts` (line 44 before D3, line 43 now) fetches history only when `playedPairsSet` is missing, which production never does; (5) `matchHistoryService.ts` has no unit test (0/28 lines); (6) history is fetched on every standard-mode run (`usePairingGenerator.ts:95`). Plan: C0 test-only pin (8 tests for the two functions), C1 delete the dead compat code, C2 move `matchHistoryService` to `services/matches/SchedulingHistoryService.ts` and inject the functions from the hooks (`validateMatchSchedule` gets a required second argument). Error handling stays exactly as it is (swallow and return false / []). Decision for Doug before D3: deleting the dead function removes 3 tests, which the factory integrity tool flags (only Doug can waive); the alternative is to move the dead function and its tests instead. (done in design PR D3) **(D4 update: item (3), `generateDualBlockPairings`, was deleted in "Dead code D4 result".)**
- **Remaining Slice 2/3 list is now:** D3 (scheduler history), 3D-3J (optional). (D2 `routePrefetch`: done in design PR D2.) (D3 scheduler history: done in design PR D3.)

## Design PR D2 result (built 2026-11-12; 5 commits: code, docs, hero placeholder test, wiring test, measured-results docs)

D2 removes the 14 page imports from `src/utils/routePrefetch.ts`. The nav-link hover prefetch and the idle preload of Teams, Schedule and History behave the same. No package, database or migration change.

- **What changed (code commit 07d7d88):**
  - `src/App.tsx` defines one loader per page that has a nav link (13: Index, TeamsPage, Schedule, Stats, Playoffs, History, Compare, Insights, MessageBoard, Help, Contact, AdminDashboard, Auth). Each loader is used by its `lazy(...)` line and by the map passed to the new `registerRoutePrefetchers(...)`. The call runs when the `App.tsx` module loads, before any render, so no hover or effect can run first. The other 11 lazy lines are unchanged.
  - `src/utils/routePrefetch.ts` has no page imports. It holds the registered map in a module variable. `prefetchRoute` looks the path up there: an unknown path, or a call before the map is registered, does nothing. `preloadCoreRoutes` keeps its timing (`requestIdleCallback` with a 3 s limit, else a 2 s timer) and the same three pages, now through `prefetchRoute('/teams')`, `'/schedule'` and `'/history'`.
  - The old `teamDetails` prefetch entry never had a route key, so it never ran. It is dropped. 13 live keys remain, and each maps to the same page as before (`/` Index, `/teams` TeamsPage, `/message-board` MessageBoard, `/admin` AdminDashboard, `/auth` Auth, and `/schedule`, `/stats`, `/playoffs`, `/history`, `/compare`, `/insights`, `/help`, `/contact` to the page of the same name).
- **Tests:** 2 files changed; none skipped or removed. `src/utils/__tests__/routePrefetch.test.ts` is rewritten: 17 tests and 5 `expect` lines before, 19 tests and 16 `expect` lines after. It drops the page mock and uses fake loaders. It checks that nothing happens before a map is registered (a fresh copy of the module, so test order does not matter), that each of the 13 keys calls its own loader once and no other, that an unknown path calls none, that a failed load raises no unhandled rejection (from a hover and from the idle preload), that the idle preload runs exactly `/teams`, `/schedule` and `/history` (with `{ timeout: 3000 }`, and not before the idle moment), and that the 2 s timer does the same where `requestIdleCallback` is missing. A scratch edit that removed the `.catch` made both "swallows" tests fail, and it was restored. A `vi.fn` spy hides the rejection (it attaches its own handler), so those two tests use a plain function as the failing loader. `src/__tests__/admin-gating.test.tsx` gained `registerRoutePrefetchers: vi.fn()` in its `@/utils/routePrefetch` mock. `Navbar.test.tsx` and `NavLinks.test.tsx` mock only `prefetchRoute` and needed no change. Run in `routePrefetch.test.ts`, `src/__tests__`, `src/components/layout`, `src/components/navigation` and `src/pages`: 39 files, 387 tests before; 39 files, 389 tests after (387 - 17 + 19).
- **Honest limit (and how it was closed):** the `routePrefetch` unit test proves the lookup and the timing. It does NOT prove that `App.tsx` maps each route to the right page (a swapped pair such as `'/stats': loadPlayoffs` would pass). QA proved the wiring once with a static check of the built app; the new `appRoutePrefetchWiring.test.ts` now pins it permanently (a swapped pair, a wrong page, a separate inline `lazy`, or a deleted key makes it fail). A new prefetch route needs one new row in that test's `wiring` table.
- **Counts (measured by QA):**
  - **Import graph:** wrong-way import edges 17 -> 3 (14 removed, 0 added). The 3 left are `matchHistoryService.ts`, `compatibilityUtils.ts` and the `useToast.ts` type import. Import loops 0 -> 0.
  - **Unit tests:** full suite 853 files, 7,848 -> 7,850 tests at the D2 code commit. The two test files added afterwards hold 59 tests (3 in `HeroCardSkeleton.test.tsx`, 56 in `appRoutePrefetchWiring.test.ts`), so the suite is now 855 files and 7,909 tests. That total is the sum of the scoped runs: the full suite was not re-run.
  - **Build:** main entry 247,562 -> 243,659 bytes raw (-3,903); gzip 78,077 -> 76,549. All 13 page chunks and all other chunks are content-identical: 331 of 332 non-map files are identical once hash suffixes are ignored, and only the main entry differs. 113 other chunks were renamed only because they import the main entry.
  - **Route to chunk map:** the 13 route-to-chunk rows are identical in the built app. A mutant with `/stats` and `/playoffs` swapped was flagged by the checker. 11 deliberate code breaks each failed at least one `routePrefetch` test.
  - **Real browser:** chunk requests are identical at both commits. Idle preload loads the Index, TeamsPage, Schedule and History chunks, and not Stats or Playoffs. Hover, focus and touch on the 9 top-nav links, Admin and the 4 bottom-nav links each fetch their own page. `/compare`, `/insights` and `/auth` have no prefetching nav link, so only the built map proves them.

Both pass 3/3 when run alone. **KNOWN FLAKE, not caused by D2:** they flake the same way at baseline under repeated 4-worker load with a cold Vite cache (3 of 20 and 2 of 20 at baseline, 2 of 20 at HEAD).

It reads `App.tsx` as text and fails on a swapped pair, a loader that imports the wrong page, an inline `lazy(...)` that skips the shared loader, and a missing key.

## Design PR D3 result (built 2026-11-13; commits: pin tests, delete dead code, move and inject, docs)

D3 removes the last util -> service imports in `src/utils/autoSchedule/`. The Auto Schedule screen (the league-night path) behaves the same: same history lookups, same swallowed errors, same warnings. No package, database or migration change.

- **Commits:** 12dc049 (pin tests), 1c0b9d8 (delete dead code), a0e7eaa (move and inject), and the docs commit that holds this section.
- **What changed:**
  - **Pin (12dc049):** 9 new tests for the two history functions, which had none. No production edit.
  - **Delete (1c0b9d8):** `haveTeamsPlayed` and `checkTeamsPlayedHistory` in `src/utils/autoSchedule/compatibilityUtils.ts` (no production caller) and their 3 tests. The `MatchReadService` barrel lost `checkTeamsEverPlayed` (nothing imported it through the barrel; the function stayed in `MatchHistoryService.ts` then; now removed in D3a, as is that file's any-season `haveTeamsPlayedBefore`; `SchedulingHistoryService.haveTeamsPlayedBefore` is the live one and stays).
  - **Move and inject (a0e7eaa):** `src/utils/autoSchedule/matchHistoryService.ts` is now `src/services/matches/SchedulingHistoryService.ts`, a git rename at 100% (text unchanged; `cmp` against the D3-1 file agrees). Its test moved to `src/services/matches/__tests__/SchedulingHistoryService.test.ts` (99% rename: only the import path changed; still 9 tests). `validateMatchSchedule`, `calculateScheduleHealth` and the private `checkForRematches` in `validation.ts` take the history function as a **required last argument**; `useEditableMatches.ts` (2 calls) and `useAutoScheduleSave.ts` (1 call) pass `haveTeamsPlayedBefore` from the new service. `TeamPairingConfig.fetchSeasonHistoryFn` (required) replaces the dead `haveTeamsPlayedFn`; `src/hooks/scheduling/utils/standardPairing.ts` passes `fetchSeasonHistoryForTeams`; `blossom/generatePairings.ts` calls `config.fetchSeasonHistoryFn(teamIds)` in its prefetch branch; `dualBlock/pairingGenerator.ts` takes the function as a parameter before the optional `notifyCallback`. The `matchHistoryService` exception block is deleted from `eslint.config.js` (only the `useToast` block stays), `ARCHITECTURE.md` → Layer rules lists one exception file, and the stale comments in `MatchHistoryService.ts` are corrected.
  - **Docs (this commit):** this section.
- **DOUG'S DECISION: "D3, delete" (given 2026-11-12).** He approved deleting `haveTeamsPlayed` and `checkTeamsPlayedHistory` with their 3 tests (4 `expect` lines) instead of moving them. He waives the factory integrity finding "`src/utils/autoSchedule/__tests__/compatibilityUtils.test.ts`: 4 fewer assertions" for this change only.
- **Tests (none skipped or weakened; the only deletion is the approved one):**
  - **D3-0:** +9 (4 for `haveTeamsPlayedBefore`, 5 for `fetchSeasonHistoryForTeams`). A deliberate break survived the first version of two of them, so those two also check the one logged error.
  - **D3-1:** -3 (the approved deletion).
  - **D3-2 (measured):** `src/utils/autoSchedule`, `src/hooks/scheduling`, `src/hooks/useAutoSchedule` and `src/services/matches`: 39 files, 422 tests before; 40 files, 430 tests after (+1 file, +8). The wider run (adds `src/hooks/__tests__/usePairingGenerator.test.ts`, `src/components/admin/auto-schedule`, `src/components/admin/batch-matches`): 57 files, 550 tests before; 58 files, 559 after (+1 file, +9). The 9 new tests: 2 in `blossomPairingAlgorithm.test.ts` (the history function is called when rematches are avoided and no played-pairs set is given, and is not called when a set is given); 3 in the new `src/hooks/scheduling/utils/__tests__/standardPairing.test.ts` (the played-pairs set is built from the fetched pairs with a sorted key, the config carries `fetchSeasonHistoryForTeams`, the rematch setting passes through); 1 in `usePairingGenerator.test.ts` (the fetched history is the third argument of `scheduleStandardPairings`); 1 in `useAutoScheduleSave.test.ts` and 2 in `generateConflictEdit.integration.test.tsx` (identity checks: `validateMatchSchedule` gets `(matches, haveTeamsPlayedBefore)` from `SchedulingHistoryService`). The moved test keeps its 9. One extra `expect` sits in an existing case in `src/utils/autoSchedule/__tests__/validation.test.ts` (the passed function is called with the pair's ids).
- **Wiring proof (throwaway copy, one break at a time; each break failed the named test and the copy was removed):**

| Break                                                                                                           | Failing test                                                                                                     |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `useAutoScheduleSave.ts` imports the `MatchHistoryService` function                                             | `useAutoScheduleSave.test.ts`: "validates with the current-season rematch lookup..."                             |

(The two table rows about `fetchSeasonHistoryFn` and the "prefetch even when a set is given" break are history. D6 removed that field and its prefetch branch, so those three tests are gone too.)

- **Name clash (do NOT merge; rename in a later slice):** the new `SchedulingHistoryService.haveTeamsPlayedBefore` checks the **current season** and **swallows errors** (false). `MatchHistoryService.haveTeamsPlayedBefore` checks **any season** and **throws**. Same name, same signature, different meaning, so typecheck cannot catch a wrong auto-import. The identity assertions in the save-path tests guard against it. The comment above `MatchHistoryService.haveTeamsPlayedBefore` now says so.
- **Left over (not done on purpose):**
  - `src/utils/autoSchedule/cachingUtils.ts` is now used only by its own test. (Deleted with its test in dead-code PR D2.)
  - `generateDualBlockPairings` has no production caller. Deleting it would remove about 10 more tests and needs a new waiver: follow-up only. Its `fetchSeasonHistoryFn` argument is guarded by typecheck only (QA: acceptable for a function nobody calls). **Done in D4: the function, its file and its tests were deleted (see "Dead code D4 result").**
  - **From QA (low, existing gaps):** (1) `calculateScheduleHealth` takes the new history argument, but no test proves it is used (a no-op stub survives); the function has no production caller. Add one rematch-stub case (expect health 95) or delete the function in a later slice. (2) The dual-mode forward of `historyPairs` in `usePairingGenerator.ts` (`scheduleDualBlockPairings`) is pinned by no test; the same break also survived at baseline. **Done in D4: `calculateScheduleHealth` was deleted with its 3 tests.**
  - `MatchHistoryService.haveTeamsPlayedBefore` (the any-season twin) has no non-test caller: a candidate for deletion in the rename slice. (Deleted in dead-code PR D3a.)
- **Deliberate exception to a CLAUDE.md rule:** CLAUDE.md says services always throw. The moved file returns `false` / `[]` on errors. It is kept byte for byte so the league-night behaviour does not change. Lint does not enforce the rule.
- **Findings for Slice 6 (existing behaviour, out of scope):**
  - History is fetched on every Auto Schedule run, also in dual mode (`usePairingGenerator.ts` fetches before it picks the mode).
  - The count includes completed playoff matches: `fetchMatchPairsInSeason` and `countTeamMatchesInSeason` filter by season and `iscompleted` only, with no bracket filter.
  - If the history lookup fails on league night, the app shows no rematch warning and avoids no rematches. The functions return `false` / `[]` on purpose.
- **Counts:** remaining known wrong-way imports: **1** (the `useToast` type import, a documented exception). Wrong-way edges 3 -> 1 (VERIFIED by QA with the import-graph script; import loops stay 0). No production file under `src/utils/autoSchedule/` imports `@/services/...`.
- **Checks (implementer, at D3-2):** typecheck, knip, whole-repo `npm run lint` (0 messages) and `npm run build` exit 0; `grep -rn "matchHistoryService" src` finds nothing; `grep -rln "@/services" src/utils/autoSchedule --include=*.ts | grep -v __tests__` finds nothing.
- **Interruption:** the container restarted during D3-2. The work was finished from the saved working tree: audited line by line against the plan, gates and wiring proof re-run, then committed.
- **Remaining Slice 2/3 list is now:** 3D-3J (optional). `hooks/useToast.ts` stays as the documented type-only exception.

## Tests-only PR for the DeepSource coverage lines (follow-up to #1738), built 2026-11-13

This PR adds tests only. It changes no source line, no package, no database and no migration. Nothing to hand-apply.

- **Why:** the `DeepSource: Test coverage` status was red on #1738. QA proved #1738 made no file worse. The same statements were uncovered on `main` in all 13 files, and overall line coverage went from 89.01% to 89.32%. `main` was green for that status. The status is a legacy commit status, not a required check, so #1738 merged.
- **What DeepSource listed:** 132 lines in 13 files.
  - 71 lines are in two files that #1738 moved: `nativeAuth.ts` (26) and `teamLoaderUtils.ts` (45). A diff without rename detection shows a moved file as a whole-file add.
  - 61 lines are old code in files where #1738 changed one import line.
  - Why DeepSource flags these 13 files and not the other 35 touched files that also have gaps: **UNKNOWN**.
- **Doug's decisions (2026-11-13):** "merge now, tests later", then "tests PR". Dead code is **not tested and not deleted** in this PR.
- **Commits:** 13 test commits on top of `main` `d16f9ba` (`78845b3` to `a70f2db`), one review-fix test commit ("Test: tighten wording and drop two soft pins found in review", see "Scoring review" below), and two docs commits (one holds this section). 12 test files changed: 10 edited, 2 new (`nativeAuth.test.ts`, `useMatchPrediction.test.tsx`). The only removed lines are 3 import lines that were rewritten. No `expect` was removed.
- **Rule for every new test:** it pins today's behaviour and its name says so. None of them states a league rule. None asserts the Power Score weights, the 0.85 default or the `sos: 0.5` / `power_score: 0` defaults. The stat numbers in the tests are made-up inputs.

- **Looks wrong, NOT fixed here, needs a later decision.** Each item is VERIFIED by the implementer who found it (items 1, 2: Run 2; items 3, 4: Run 3; items 4b, 5, 6: Run 1). No test pins any of them as a rule. None needs a league-rule answer except where marked.
  1. `standardPairing.ts:38-41` skips a block with one team using `continue`. That lone team is never added to `unmatchedTeamIds`, so it is silently left out. The odd-block warning (line 45) says "One team will be unmatched", but a 3-team block can reach 2 matches for every team, so nobody is unmatched. The comment at line 43 says "Skip blocks with odd number", but the code only warns. This is the league-night scheduling path: **ask Doug before any change.** **BUILT 2026-11-13 (PR-S, see "PR-S result"):** a lone team is now counted as unmatched, and an odd block of 3 or more stops Generate with a clear message in standard mode. The line numbers above are from before PR-S.
  2. `teamLoaderUtils.ts:152-155` catches any error and returns `[]`, so one bad row drops the whole pair's load. Lines 18-19 (bad date), 24-25 (bad pair name) and 44-47 (fetch error) also return `[]`: callers see "no teams", not an error. CLAUDE.md says services throw. Lines 126-129 cannot run with real rows, because line 78 already drops rows without `teams`.
  3. `nativeAuth.ts:18-26` falls back to `serverAuthCode`, then `accessToken`, then `token`, and sends the value to `signInWithIdToken`. `nativeAuth.ts:51` logs `JSON.stringify(response)`, the whole Google response, which may put tokens in the console. This matters only if a native app ever ships (the repo has none).
  4. `useMatchPrediction.ts:128` treats any `winnerId` that is not team 1 as team 2 (it uses `probB`). `useMatchPrediction.ts:64` counts an explicit `null` prefetched head-to-head as "provided", so the hook skips the per-card lookup and passes no head-to-head to the predictor. `TimeSlotMatchGroup.tsx:84` passes `getHeadToHead(...)`, which can return `null` (INFERRED: while the batch loads, or when there is no record). A prediction card may then show without head-to-head weighting on league night. This is scoring-adjacent: **ask Doug.** When the weights query fails, the hook returns `prediction: null` with `isLoading` false and no error. The doc comment above the hook still says "65% Career + 25% Current Season + 10% H2H", while `docs/agents/LEAGUE_CONTEXT.md` does not list the 65/25/10 prediction split at all (its lines 63-64 say the division weights are unconfirmed).
  - 4b. `useTeamOperations.handleLoadTeams`: after a load that returns no teams, it still stores the empty map and sets `teamsLoadedDate`. Nobody is sure that is intended. The new test pins the warning and the empty map, but not `teamsLoadedDate`.
  5. `usePlayoffLoserSwap.ts:50` calls `refetchQueries` after `invalidateQueries`, which may be redundant. The `useMemo` in `usePlayoffViewModel.compat.ts` carries an `eslint-disable` for its dependencies (line 124) and captures the first `refetchBrackets` (stable today).
  6. `useTeamScheduleLoader.loadTeamsForDate(date, true)` with no dual-block config behaves like normal mode and resets the paired map. Not pinned. **Done in D5: the dual parameters and the paired map were removed from the loader.**
- **Follow-ups:** the "Looks wrong" list above, the dead-code lines, and `usePlayoffLoserSwap.ts:59-60` are all open. They are also listed in the carry-over notes below so they are not lost.
- **Honest limit:** DeepSource may stay red. Its rule is unknown. The dead-code lines are the reason it may stay red. A later dead-code PR in the Slice 1 style can remove them. Deleting tests there needs Doug's waiver. Watch both DeepSource statuses after the push.

### Scoring review (read-only specialist, 2026-11-13)

- **Result:** no new test freezes a scoring rule; nothing reverted. The review asked for small fixes. Every fix touches a test that this unpushed branch added, so no test in `main` is weakened. One test commit holds them. No source line changed.
- **Fixes made (tests only):**
  - `useMatchPrediction.test.tsx`: the mocked `isUpset` said "upset" for the 80% favourite and "no upset" for the 20% underdog. That was backwards. The answers are swapped. The test still proves that the hook asks `isUpset` about the winner's probability (team 1, then team 2) and returns the answer. It does not assert the real upset rule. Two comments say the fixtures are made-up values and the real predictor uses a 0-100 Power Score scale.
  - `weeklyTrendsForWeek.test.ts`: the empty-week test no longer pins `basis: 'compared'` and `previousWeek: 5`. That result is doubtful (open question 4). It still pins the empty trends, the week number, and that team details are never read. Source line 111 stays covered. **BUILT 2026-11-13 (PR-R):** open question 4 is answered, so this test now pins `basis: 'missing'` and `previousWeek: null` too (see "PR-R result").
  - `standardPairing.test.ts`: the check "no warning for a team with no matches" is gone, and the test title no longer says it. The unmatched list stays. Two comments say that the odd-block test pins only what the function returns, and that the repeated pairs in the zero-match test are mock fixtures.
  - `teamLoaderUtils.test.ts`: a comment says the three pairs come from the mocked constants and the real list has nine.
  - `useTeamOperations.test.ts`: the mocked loader returns every pair key with an empty list, like the real loader.
- **Net effect on `main`:** no `expect` is removed from `main`. One `expect` that this branch itself added is gone (`standardPairing.test.ts`). The factory integrity scan, run against `main`, finds no weakened test.
- **Break re-check after the fixes:** the implementer re-ran the deliberate breaks that touch the edited tests against the edited tests: `weeklyTrendsForWeek` (5a-5e), `standardPairing` (S1-S5), `useTeamOperations` (4a, 4b), `teamLoaderUtils` (T8a, T8b), and the `useMatchPrediction` missing-data and upset-flag breaks (B3, B18-B21). All were caught except 5e and S5. Those two are not pinned on purpose: each can only be caught by pinning a doubtful result. (Update 2026-11-13: PR-R settled the result behind 5e. `previousWeek: null` for a week with no scored team is pinned now.)
- **Two INFERRED points (read from code, not run on a real league night):**
  - In standard mode, an odd block builds a 3-team triangle (the generator targets 2 matches per team, `generatePairings.ts:30-31`). A triangle cannot sit in two slots, because every pair of its matches shares a team. The save step puts the third match back in slot 0 and double-books a team (`usePairingOperations.ts:294-300`). The code comment above it says validation refuses a double-booked team (`usePairingOperations.ts:285-288`). So the save step is expected to refuse an odd block. **VERIFIED 2026-11-13 (PR-S pins):** for 3, 7 and 9 teams Generate and Apply raised no complaint and `validateMatchSchedule` refused the night ("Team is scheduled for multiple matches at 6:30 PM"). 5 teams of one division failed earlier, at Generate, with a plain error. PR-S now stops all of them at Generate.
  - `docs/product-description/admin/build-the-schedule.md:278-279` says an odd block "leaves one team unmatched". That matches dual mode, where `handleOddTeamCount` removes one team (`dualBlockUtils.ts:66`). ## Unrated division result (career bonus), built 2026-11-16 (migration file `20261117120000`)

An unknown division is **unrated** in the career bonus: it adds 0 instead of counting as 0.85. TypeScript and SQL give the same number, except for a division name with a leading tab or a non-breaking space: TypeScript rates it and SQL leaves it unrated (see the migration header and fixture 13). **The SQL was applied to the live database by Doug on 2026-11-16, before PR #1744 was merged** (`docs/OPERATIONS.md` section 6f). The fingerprint (count and md5 of `calculate_career_power_score` over all teams) was **identical before and after**: count 51, md5 `c4d29aa83f550d893309275afc806e5f` (Doug's screenshots; the SQL Editor reported "Success. No rows returned"). The resolver check gave 1.00 for `Competitive` and NULL for `zzz`. The optional `has_function_privilege` check was not reported back. No number changes today (live read 2026-11-13: every division has a weight, every past division name matches).

- **The rule (the same in both places).**
  - Unrated = a blank name, a name that matches no division, or a matched division with a NULL weight. A weight of 0 is a real 0.
  - A matched division with a NULL weight stops the lookup. It does not fall through to a synthetic label or a base tier word.
  - Title and runner-up: an unrated one adds 0 (the champion count still shows).
  - Playoff win-rate weight: the average over rated seasons only. With none rated it uses the team's current weight. If that is unrated too, the win-rate bonus is 0. The win rate still counts every playoff match. **This fallback is a developer choice (the simplest rule; the bonus is worth about 2 points or less). Doug can overrule it.**
  - Bonus cap: 15 x (the highest rated weight)^2. With none rated it uses the team weight. If that is unrated too, the whole bonus is 0.
  - The team's own weight has no default: `number | null`, and a stored 0 stays 0.
  - Not changed: the competitive-playoff count (an unknown bracket is not counted, in both places), Hidden division names.
- **Commits (branch `claude/adoring-bohr-waxxok`):** `4fe1472` pin tests (green on the old code), `7a35378` TypeScript half, `824365c` SQL (migration `20261117120000`, revert script, parity tests), `7834ae2` read-only check script, then this docs commit.
- **Files:** `src/utils/career/divisionBonusWeight.ts` (resolver returns `number | null`; rated-only average and max), `src/services/career/calculateCareerPowerScore.ts`, `CareerFetchService.ts` and `CareerBulkFetchService.ts` (no 0.85 for the team weight; **all three bulk lines changed together**), the by-name cache in `divisionWeightsCache.ts` (a null weight stays in the map as null: dropping it would let the resolver fall through to a base word and give 0.70 where SQL gives null). SQL: `supabase/migrations/20261117120000_unrated_division_career_bonus.sql`, `supabase/scripts/revert_unrated_division_bonus.sql`, `supabase/scripts/check_unrated_divisions.sql`, `supabase/tests/career_power_score_parity.sql`.
- **NOT built, and why (nothing here changes a career-bonus number):**
  - **Match predictions** (`predictMatch.ts`) keep their own 0.85. A forecast is not a bonus. Doug said to leave the prediction constants.
  - **The by-id cache** (`divisionWeightsCache.ts`, `fetchDivisionWeights`) feeds predictions only.
  - **Bracket weights** (`CareerFetchService.ts`, `CareerBulkFetchService.ts`, `calculatePlayoffStats.ts`) are only compared with 0.89. A missing weight (0.85 or null) means "not competitive" either way.
  - **The Team page tier** (`teamSeasonStats/calculations.ts`, `mappers.ts`, `fetchSeasonBreakdown.ts`) is display only.
  - **The `'Unknown'` text** in `computeAllTeamsTotals.ts` and `useTeamTotalsComputed.ts` matches no division, so the new rule already covers it.
  - **The season Power Score** already skips an unrated opponent.
  - **Id-based weights** (a weight column on each season row) need a new column and a backfill of history. Rejected.
  - **Old text copies of 0.85** in applied migrations (for example the revert text in `20260812171710`). An applied migration cannot be edited.
  - The constant `DEFAULT_DIVISION_WEIGHT` stays 0.85 for the sites above. Its comment now says the career bonus no longer uses it.
- **Two ways a division can become unknown (the reason for the check script):**
  - **A division rename.** Career weights are found by division NAME text. Archived `team_season_stats.division_name` is frozen text. Rename "Competitive" and every old Competitive title loses its weight.
  - **Free text in the History editor.** `src/services/teamSeasonStats/updates.ts:31` writes whatever name the admin types into `division_name`.
  - Run `supabase/scripts/check_unrated_divisions.sql` before a rename and after Edit Divisions on a past season. **A team with an unknown-division title or runner-up LOSES bonus points.** Doug accepts: "no proof of the division, no credit".
- **Worked example (hand-checked, then run in TypeScript and in SQL on the scratch cluster: both give the same numbers).** A team with a base score of 70.9167 (its season average). Division weights: Competitive 1.00, Intermediate 0.70, and Recreational, which exists but has no weight. The team has one Competitive title, two runner-up finishes (Competitive and Recreational), playoff seasons in Competitive, Intermediate and Recreational, a playoff record of 3 wins and 1 loss, and 1 competitive playoff win.
  - **Before:** title 7. Runner-ups 4 + 4 x 0.85^2 = 6.89 (the Recreational one counted 0.85). Win-rate weight (1.0 + 0.7 + 0.85) / 3 = 0.85, so (0.75 - 0.5) x 4 x 0.85 = 0.85. Competitive win 0.5. Sum 15.24, cut to the cap of 15. **Total 85.9167.**
  - **Now:** title 7. Runner-ups 4 + 0 = 4. Win-rate weight (1.0 + 0.7) / 2 = 0.85 (Recreational is left out), so 0.85. Competitive win 0.5. Sum 12.35, under the cap of 15. **Total 83.2667.** The team loses 2.65 points: the Recreational runner-up earns nothing.
- **KNOWN DIFFERENCE FROM SQL (not widened).** JavaScript `trim()` and `split(/\s+/)` also treat a tab and a non-breaking space as blanks. SQL `btrim()` and `split_part(' ')` do not. A name such as `<tab>Competitive` is rated 1.00 by the app and is unrated (NULL) in SQL. Both sides are pinned in tests (`divisionBonusWeight.test.ts`, "known difference from SQL"; fixture 13 and the resolver checks in `career_power_score_parity.sql`). Aligning them touches `normalize()`, the base-word split and the cache key, and would change how names rate today. Doug accepted leaving it. No such name is expected in real data.
- **Where the SQL ran.** It ran for real on a throwaway local Postgres 16 cluster (scratch folder, no network, no league data), not on Postgres 15 like CI and **not on the live database**. The steps mirrored `.github/workflows/supabase-ci.yml`: the CI bootstrap plus every migration file (443, the new one included) in one `psql`, then every file in `supabase/tests` (35 files).
  - **Before** the migration, the new parity test failed at fixture 5 (`got 55.0575`). **After**, all 35 files passed, in single files and in the CI-style combined run.
  - Fixture 5 (one unknown title, base 50): **55.0575 became 50.0000.** Fixture 6 (an Intermediate High title plus an unknown title): **58.4875 became 53.4300.** Fixtures 1-4 stayed 53.43, 57.35, 56.3175 and 0.
  - The revert script, run on a copy, gave function bodies, comments, volatility and grants identical to the state before the migration, and the old parity test passed on it. Running the migration twice gave the same functions.
  - The hand-apply fingerprint (count and md5 of every team's career score) was identical before and after on a fixture with only known divisions, and differed once one name was misspelled.
  - **Deliberate SQL breaks (each must fail a test):** resolver 0.85 restored, matched NULL falling through (exact step and alias step), title not guarded, runner-up not guarded, cap without COALESCE, playoff average counting unknown seasons, team-weight default restored, a weight of 0 treated as unrated, SQL widened to JavaScript trim, a GRANT added: all caught. A NULL bonus reaching `LEAST(100, base + NULL)` gives 100 in the guard test (fixture 10): caught. Note the unguarded-title break gives the **full cap** (65 in fixture 5), because `LEAST` skips the NULL.
  - **Two real survivors, found by QA, are now caught.** The cap built from the LOWEST rated weight (`MIN` instead of `MAX`) was not caught, because no fixture had two different rated weights. Fixture 14 now has them (Competitive 1.00 and Intermediate High 0.70): the total is 65, and `MIN` gives 57.35. A base tier word whose division has a NULL weight returning 0.85 was not caught either, because only the exact and alias steps were tested. Fixture 15 and two resolver checks now cover it (50, and NULL).
  - **Truly equivalent (no test can see them):** the final `COALESCE(v_total_playoff_bonus, 0)` removed alone (nothing can make the bonus NULL once the cap is COALESCEd, so it is a safety net), and the `v_playoff_weight IS NOT NULL` guard removed (`GREATEST(0, NULL)` is 0 in Postgres).
  - **Two more QA mutants (round 2) are now caught.** A cap that ignores the playoff-season rows gave a total no fixture could tell apart: fixture 16 (a Competitive playoff season above a title and a runner-up in Intermediate High) gives 65, and the mutant gives 57.35. The playoff weight built with `MAX` instead of the average: fixture 17 (Competitive 1.00 and Intermediate High 0.70, average 0.85) gives 51.36, and the mutant gives 51.6 (`MIN` gives 51.12, the team weight 50.8). Both fixtures use only known divisions, so the old and the new functions give the same numbers.
  - **TypeScript mirrors the SQL fixtures with the SAME inputs and totals (fixtures 1 to 17), except fixture 13.** Fixture 13 is the known difference: the TypeScript test pins 57, the SQL test pins 50.
- **Known limit (not new, not changed here): renaming a division with a MULTI-WORD name does not make its old titles unrated.** The name lookup falls through to the first word (the base tier word). Rename "Competitive Low" (0.95) to something else, and an old "Competitive Low" title now finds the division "Competitive" (1.00). The weight silently changes from 0.95 to 1.00, and **the check script cannot see it**, because the name still resolves. A single-word rename (for example "Cuspers") does become unrated and is flagged. The base-word step has been there since `20260901120000`. Doug can decide later whether a multi-word name should stop at the full name.
- **Limit of the check script.** Before the migration is applied, the old function still guesses 0.85, so the check cannot show a season finish with an unknown name. It does show teams, brackets and divisions with no weight at any time. The fingerprint comparison in section 6f is the real test of "nothing moved".
- **QA numbers (independent, round 2, HEAD `b387f80`):**
  - The full CI coverage command: baseline 874 files and 8,180 tests, HEAD 874 files and 8,230 tests. Both exit 0. The floors hold. Uncovered lines 2,509 and 2,509, and 0 files rose.
  - Typecheck, lint (0 messages), knip and build exit 0. End-to-end tests: 58 of 58.
  - 76,320 known-division scenarios: md5-identical, baseline against HEAD.
  - 12,000 random scenarios: TypeScript and SQL agree with 0 mismatches (largest difference 2.1e-14), on a real Postgres 16.15 scratch cluster.
  - All 35 SQL test files pass alone and combined. The fingerprint (count and md5) is identical before and after the migration on a 91-team live-like fixture. The revert restores the original. The hand-apply steps in section 6f ran as written.
  - Limits: CI's Postgres is 15 (QA used 16.15), and `supabase db lint` was not run (the CLI was missing).
  - Added after that round, and run by the developer on the scratch cluster: fixtures 16 and 17 in `career_power_score_parity.sql` and in `calculateCareerPowerScore.test.ts`. QA has not re-run them.
- **Done:** Doug applied the SQL (section 6f) on 2026-11-16, and PR #1744 merged the same day.
- **Open:** The playoff-fallback rule above stays a developer choice unless Doug says otherwise.

## PR-R result (recap week with no scored team), built 2026-11-13

PR-R answers open question 4 ("Recap for a week with no scored teams"). **A week whose snapshot rows have no scored team now counts as `'missing'`.** The admin sees the red warning and the Publish button is off. No SQL, no migration, nothing to hand-apply.

- **Doug's decision (2026-11-13):** a recap whose week has no scored teams counts as `'missing'`: warn, and block publishing.
- **Commits (in order):** pin tests `03400c1`, source and tests `3687db3`, warning text `7b69a44`, then the docs commit that holds this section.
- **What changed:**
  - `src/services/rankings/weeklyTrendsForWeek.ts:111-116`: when week N has rows but the scored read comes back empty, the function returns `empty(weekNumber, 'missing')`. Before, it returned `'compared'` with `previousWeek` set. `previousWeek` is `null` now, like the other `'missing'` return (`:65-70`). The comment at `:66-68` says both causes. The type comment at `src/types/powerScoreSnapshot.ts:27` says "no snapshot, or none of its rows has a power score".
  - `src/components/admin/weekly-content/PackWarnings.tsx:21`: the body of the `'missing'` warning names both causes. The old body said the job "has not run", which is wrong when the rows exist. The title is unchanged. It still says to run `capture-power-snapshots`.
  - Nothing else moved. `canPublishFacts` already blocks on `'missing'` (`buildRecapFacts.ts:162-168`). `fetchRecapFacts.ts:203-204` already reads no previous standings when `previousWeek` is `null`.
- **NOT changed, on purpose:**
  - **A scored week whose teams are all new, or all in a Hidden division, stays `'compared'` with no trends.** Both are pinned.
  - **Week 1 stays `'baseline'`.** `weeklyTrendsForWeek.ts:74-76` returns before any row is read, so a week-1 row with no score is still a baseline. Pinned by "reports a baseline when nothing earlier exists".
  - `canPublishFacts` (`buildRecapFacts.test.ts` "allows a week that has standings but no stories" stays true).
- **Tests** (all name what they pin):
  - `weeklyTrendsForWeek.test.ts`, pins first (they passed on the unchanged source): "asks only for scored rows, for this week and the week before (today's behaviour)", "stays 'compared' with no trends when every scored team is new this week (today's behaviour)", "stays 'compared' with no trends when the only scored team is in a Hidden division (today's behaviour)". The mock now records every `.not(...)` filter with its week.
  - `weeklyTrendsForWeek.test.ts`, changed on purpose: "returns no trends when this week has no snapshots (today's behaviour)" is now "calls a week missing when its rows have no scored team". It keeps every old assertion and adds `basis: 'missing'`, `previousWeek: null` and the week-6 `power_score` filter. The factory integrity scan finds nothing weakened.
  - New: "calls a week missing, not a gap, when its rows have no scored team and week 5 has no snapshot" (weeks 6 and 4: `'missing'`, not `'gap'`).
  - `fetchRecapFacts.test.ts`: "keeps a 'missing' week in the facts, reads no previous standings and blocks publishing".
  - `PackWarnings.test.tsx`: "says a missing week can also mean no row has a power score". The old title and `capture-power-snapshots` checks still pass.
- **Proof:**
  - **Deliberate breaks** (throwaway worktree, one source edit at a time): 9 of 9 caught. The three that mattered: put the old `'compared'` return back (the two new `'missing'` tests fail); make an empty trends list `'missing'` (the two "stays 'compared'" pins fail); drop the `power_score` not-null filter on the week-N read (the recording assertion fails). The other six are the gap week keeping `'gap'`, `previousWeek` kept, week 1 turned `'missing'`, a previous-standings read with no comparison week, `canPublishFacts` no longer blocking, and the old warning body.
  - Typecheck, whole-repo lint (0 messages) and knip exit 0. The three test folders (`src/services/rankings`, `src/services/recapEditions`, `src/components/admin/weekly-content`) pass. Factory integrity: "no weakened tests or suppressed checks found".
- **Honest limits:**

That function has two forms in migration `20260812171710_392b5c8f-8cd3-4d71-ab90-7448b15e243e.sql`. The unified form (lines 798-831) returns `power_score_100(...)`, which wraps every input in `COALESCE` (the live definition is regenerated in migration `20260820183328_de2e779d-97d2-4d26-b0a2-e291ede8511f.sql:118-120`; an older hard-coded 40/45/15 form is in `20260811142827_b85b55be-14eb-4921-8ab1-a40bda3e2de7.sql:144-146`), and keeps only teams with `c.matches_played > 0` (line 831). The pre-unification form (lines 487-613) gives NULL only for a team with no matches, and keeps only `match_counts.total_matches > 0` (line 613). So under both forms a returned team has a score. Which form is live was not re-read. So this closes a hole for odd or hand-written rows only. A normal week cannot trigger it: a bye week, a playoff week, the first week or a Hidden team. (INFERRED for the bye week and playoff week: the scores are season to date, so such a week still has scored rows. Not run against live data.)

- **Rollback:** revert the commits, newest first. No database change.

## PR-S result (standard-mode scheduler: blocks of 1, 2 and odd sizes), built 2026-11-13

PR-S answers open questions 1 and 2 and fixes one more bug found on the way (a block of exactly 2 teams made Generate fail for the whole night). It changes only what **standard mode** (Dual Match Mode off) does. Dual mode, the greedy scheduler, Apply, Save validation and saved matches are not touched. No SQL, no migration, nothing to hand-apply.

- **Doug's decisions (2026-11-13):**
  - **A block with one team:** the lone team is counted as unmatched, and named on screen.
  - **A block of exactly 2 teams:** one match between the two. They are not unmatched.
  - **An odd block of 3 or more in standard mode:** he first said "one team sits out", then "nobody sits out, extra match at the next half-hour". The skeptic found that **Dual Match Mode (on by default) already does exactly that**: the block gets a third slot at the next half-hour (`dualBlockScheduler.ts:87-88`, `scheduleOdd.ts:84`). Doug uses Dual mode. **Final: standard mode stops early with a clear message.** No new engine.
- **Commits (in order):**
  - **Run 1:** pins `58befaf` (even-block output through the real generator), lone team and de-dupe `a871fbf`, block of 2 `44a8ec6`.
  - **Run 2:** pins `e79d207`, early stop `1956868`, messages `a385029`, then the docs commit that holds this section.
- **What changed:**
  - **Lone team (`standardPairing.ts:65`):** a block with exactly 1 team adds that team to `unmatchedTeamIds`. An empty block adds nothing. The final list is de-duplicated once at return (`:128`), in first-seen order. This is the only change to even-block output: a team short in two blocks is listed once, not twice.
  - **Block of 2 (`generatePairings.ts:31` and `standardPairing.ts:96`):** the target is `Math.min(2, teams.length - 1)`, so two teams get one match and are not flagged. Sizes 3 and up keep a target of 2.
  - **Early stop (`standardPairing.ts:32-47`):** before any block is generated, the function looks at ALL blocks. If any has an odd count of 3 or more it throws ONE `ValidationError` and generates nothing. The message names every odd block by its time label (`BACK_TO_BACK_PAIRS` in `constants.ts`) and team count: "Block Early Pair (6:30-7:00 PM) has 5 teams. Standard mode needs an even number of teams in a block. Move a team, or use Dual Match Mode on the Auto Schedule screen." Two odd blocks give one message. The error details keep the raw keys and counts. A block with no label falls back to its key. The old odd-block console warning is gone.
  - **How the message reaches the admin (VERIFIED by tests, both screens):** both screens call `generateMatchPairings` in `usePairingGenerator.ts`. Its catch (`:141-148`) calls `getUIErrorMessage`, which keeps a `ValidationError` message (`errorHandler.ts:237-241`), and shows the red toast "Failed to generate match pairings: ..." On **Auto Schedule** the function then returns `null`, and `usePairingOperations.ts:234-239` adds a second, generic "Generation Failed" toast. `TOAST_LIMIT` is 3 (`useToast.ts:10`), so both stay on screen. On **Match Creation**, `useSchedulePreview.handleGenerateSchedule` has no catch of its own and gets `null`: only the first toast shows. A team alone in its time block has nobody to play." (`useSchedulePreview.ts:205-210`). The preview copy no longer promises that an odd block leaves one team out: `WarningDisplay.tsx` (titles kept), `TeamLoadingStep.tsx` (only a lone team is listed as unmatched; a new "Blocks to Fix" line for odd blocks of 3 or more), `InformationSection.tsx`, `InfoFooter.tsx`, and the comments and one console warning in `edgeCaseUtils.ts` (its functions return the same values).

"still needs two matches per team in a 3-team block" is now "... in a block of 4" (a 3-team block can no longer reach that check; the same target of 2 is pinned on 4 teams). The odd-block pins above now expect the early stop. Run 1 changed two tests on purpose in `a871fbf`: "skips a block with fewer than two teams ..." (it now also counts the lone team) and "lists a team that is short in two blocks once" (it said "twice").
