## Pairing retry result (commits 1 and 2 of the six-team Generate fix), built 2026-11-14

Standard mode (Dual Match Mode off) threw on about 1 in 10 blocks of 6, 8 or 10 teams in the Node tests, and on about 1 in 5 blocks in a real browser (18.4% of the mixed grid; see "Browser finding"). No history is needed. At least two division labels are needed (0 of the 258 throws have a single division label; 49 have no history). Doug asked for the fix on the same PR (#1741). Dual mode and the greedy scheduler are not touched. No SQL, no migration, nothing to hand-apply.

- **Cause (VERIFIED):** `matchingRunner.ts` called `edmondsBlossom(edgeList)` without `maxCardinality`. Each round was a maximum-weight matching, not a perfect one, so a heavy pair could beat two lighter pairs. Round 1 left teams out or used an edge round 2 needed, and the repair step could not undo a pair.
- **Change (commit 1):** `generatePairingsWithBlossom` runs the generator as before. Only when that run throws, for an even block of 4 or more teams, it runs once more with perfect matchings (`runBlossomMatching(..., maxCardinality = true)`). The second list is used only if `usesOnlyAllowedEdges` is true: no Competitive-vs-Recreational pair, and no rematch unless both teams are Recreational. If it breaks a rule, or the second run throws too, the **first error is thrown unchanged**. Odd blocks, blocks of 2 and the default `maxCardinality = false` behave exactly as before. A `warnLog` marks the runs that use the second list.
- **No new league rule and no new relaxation.** Doug's rules stay as they were: a list that needs a rematch (except Recreational) or a Competitive-vs-Recreational pair is still refused.
- **Numbers from the QA grid (2,901 saved seeded scenarios, real Blossom, brute-force check for a rule-following schedule):** 258 throws. 243 of them (242 seeded scenarios plus the QA 6-team case) have a schedule that breaks no rule. The other 15 need a rematch (7) or a Competitive-vs-Recreational pair (8). In 12 of the 15 the second run returns a list that breaks a rule, so the check refuses it. In the 3 others the second run throws too. All 15 keep today's error. **Confirmed by QA in Node mode: 2,429 of 2,429 working nights are byte-identical, 243 of 243 throws with a rule-following schedule are fixed, and the 15 others throw the same message.**
- **Pinned in `generatePairings.retry.test.ts` and `matchingRunner.test.ts` (commit 1):** the pins were written first against the unchanged code. Changed on purpose here: the four "rejects" pins (the QA 6-team case, n6-15, n6-14, n6-177) are now "returns a rule-following list" with the exact pairs. Not changed: the two lists Apply cannot split, the six working nights, blocks of 0, 1, 2, 3 and 5, and the three blocks with no rule-following schedule (still the same error text).
- **Commit 2 (the unsplittable lists):** a second trigger for the same retry. When the first run returns a list that Apply cannot split into the block's 2 slots, the generator runs the perfect-matching retry and uses it only if `usesOnlyAllowedEdges` AND `splitsIntoTwoSlots` are both true. Otherwise it returns the first list unchanged and writes one `warnLog` ("pairings cannot be split into 2 slots and no rule-following alternative was found"). A used replacement also writes a `warnLog`. The throw path is unchanged: it does not ask for a splittable list. Odd blocks, blocks of 2 and a first list that splits are not touched.
  - **`splitsIntoTwoSlots` copies Apply's rule** (`usePairingOperations.ts`, `handleApplySchedule`): 2 slots, list order, each pair into the first slot where neither team is booked yet, no free slot means false. A false "no" would replace a list that works, so `splitsIntoTwoSlots.applyAgreement.test.ts` runs the real Apply on 300 fixed pseudo-random lists (6 and 8 teams: valid lists, order-only failures, odd cycles, copied pairs, partial lists, 2 or 3 pairs per team) and the helper agrees with Apply on all 300.
  - **Numbers on the saved mixed grid (2,901 scenarios): QA confirmed in Node mode:** 214 unsplittable lists, 123 replaced (n6 79, n8 23, n10 21), 91 unchanged (69 = 54 rule-following but unsplittable + 15 rule-breaking and unsplittable, plus 22 where the retry splits but breaks a rule). The earlier QA figure of 145 had no rule check; the check refuses those 22.
  - **Pins changed on purpose:** the n6-3 (two triangles) and n6-1 (wrong order) pins now return the new rule-following lists. New: n8-17 and n10-2 are replaced; n6-1030, n6-1124 and n6-1286 (a 6-cycle in the wrong order, same list from the second run), n6-781 (the second list puts a Competitive team against a Recreational team) and n6-52 (the second list needs a rematch) return the first list unchanged.
- **Leftovers (nothing changed for these; no new league rule):**
  - **26 cases with a rule-following schedule that the retry does not find** (QA count at HEAD): 19 have the right pairs in the wrong order and 7 are two triangles. They stay as they were, so Doug can still fix them by hand in the editable preview. An exact search (try every first round, about 100 lines more and a time limit) would fix them. Not built. A possible follow-up.
  - **A block whose first run throws and that has no rule-following schedule still stops, with the same error.** 238 of 1,501 mixed-grid six-team blocks have no rule-following schedule. For most of them the first run already hands back a list that bends a rule, and this change leaves that list byte-identical. Only the blocks where the first run THROWS still stop: 11 of 1,501 six-team blocks, 15 of 2,901 over all sizes (Node), and 4.1% of the six-team blocks in the clean grid. Open question for Doug, recommendation: keep stopping. Should Generate allow one flagged rematch there?
  - **116 working nights use a rematch or a Competitive-vs-Recreational pair although a rule-following schedule exists** (QA count). The first run is kept byte-identical on purpose, so these are not changed.
  - **A scoring question, not touched:** `compatibilityUtils.ts:10-11` scores a team with no division, or a division name that has no "competitive", "intermediate" or "recreational" in it (for example Cuspers), as Recreational (`getDisplayDivision` fallback, `divisions.ts:107`). `tierUtils.ts:7` and `:16` treat the same teams as Tier 2. So the score and the tier rule disagree for those teams.
  - **Match Creation, not touched:** it alternates a block's two times by row (`matchConversionUtils.ts:119`), and its form has a limit of one row per team (from the plan, not re-checked here). Those limits are not the same as Apply's first-fit rule on Auto Schedule.

## Browser finding (library crash), found by QA 2026-11-14

- **What happens (VERIFIED by QA in a real browser and in a production build):** `edmonds-blossom` 1.0.0 (`app/blossom.js`) assigns three variables it never declares: `b` and `path` in `addBlossom`, `v` in `expandBlossom`. Node runs the file as sloppy CommonJS, so those become silent globals and every test passes. A browser bundle is a strict ES module, so the original throws `ReferenceError: b is not defined` as soon as a round needs a blossom contraction. `matchingRunner.ts` catches the error, the round returns no pairs, and the greedy repair takes over. The retry from the first two commits crashed the same way, so it did not help in a browser.
- **How big it is (QA, 2,901-scenario mixed grid, browser mode):** 535 blocks throw (18.4%), against 258 in Node. For 4-team blocks, 31 of 400 throw in the browser and 0 in Node. The browser result differs from the Node result for 2,014 of 2,901 scenarios (69%). Dual Match Mode is not affected: it uses the greedy engine.
- **Decision (Doug, 2026-11-14): fix only the retry, with a strict-mode copy of the library** (`src/utils/autoSchedule/blossom/vendor/edmondsBlossomStrict.js`: MIT licence kept in the header, ES module export, `var b, path;` and `var v;` declared, nothing else changed). `runBlossomMatching` uses the copy when `maxCardinality` is true (the retry) and the original otherwise. **The first run keeps the original library, so working nights stay byte-identical, in Node and in a browser.** Tests: the copy equals the original on 400 seeded graphs; the original source run in strict mode still throws (when upstream is fixed that test fails, and the copy can go); a simulated browser (the original throws on every call) still gets a rule-following, splittable list through the retry, and the no-rule-following blocks still throw the same error.
- **OPEN ITEM, Doug's call, not built:** fix the library for every night. About 2 of 3 standard-mode nights would then get different (valid) pairings, so it changes what admins see on a working night. Recommendation: decide later. Delete the vendored copy when upstream fixes the library.
- **QA browser results (real production build, baseline against HEAD):** the QA 6-team block went from the red error to "Schedule Generated", with 0 clashes and 2 matches per team. 19 blocks were compared: the working ones are identical, 4 of 4 fixable blocks are fixed, and 3 of 3 blocks with no rule-following schedule give the same red toasts. Browser emulation over the mixed grid: 1,459 of 1,459 working nights are byte-identical; 535 baseline throws became 509 fixed and 26 the same throw; 907 unsplittable lists became 742 replaced and 165 unchanged.
- **Finding p (QA, corrected):** the throw path accepts a retry list that follows the rules, and does not also ask for a splittable list. In Node and on the browser mixed grid that never returned an unsplittable list (0 cases). **On the browser CLEAN grid it did in 4 of 1,900 scenarios** (n8-215, n10-236, n10-278, n10-282). Those blocks have odd-sized Competitive and Recreational groups and no Intermediate team (for example 7 Competitive and 3 Recreational). Such a block has NO schedule that is both rule-following and splittable (an odd group of strict opponents forms an odd cycle). The admin sees "Schedule Generated", then a clash of 2 at Export, and fixes it by hand in the editable preview.
  - **Decision (Lead, not a league rule): keep it.** A rule-following list the admin can edit is better than the red error that blocks the night.
  - **Alternative, NOT built:** make the throw path also require a splittable list. Those 4 of 1,900 clean-grid blocks would then show the red error again.

Each fails.

## Slice 6 result (built 2026-11-16 on `claude/adoring-bohr-waxxok`; C1 was built and then UNDONE the same day; QA PASSed the first build, the later changes below need a new QA run)

Doug decided on 2026-11-16 (he took every recommendation). **Later the same day he corrected C1 (see C1 below).** **Rule for the slice: no number changes beyond the three decisions below and their listed knock-ons. No SQL, no migration, nothing to hand-apply. Nothing stored changes: all three numbers are worked out when the page opens. Rollback: revert the commits.**

- **C1 (`d325bc1`): BUILT, then UNDONE the same day (commit `f062316`, "Undo: leave games against Hidden teams out of the Team-page split (wrong rule)"). Do not build it again.**
  - **What was built:** `categorizeDivision` in `src/services/teamSeasonStats/calculations.ts` returned null for a division name that contains "hidden", so the "vs Competitive / Intermediate / Recreational" cards left those games out, like Career. Doug had taken the recommendation.
  - **Why it was wrong (Doug, 2026-11-16, from his live data):** Hidden is where an admin moves a team AFTER it drops out. The move overwrites the old division with no history (header of `supabase/migrations/20260812130000_power_score_historical_opponent_division.sql`, lines 9-15). So a game against a team that is Hidden now was a real game, and the opponent was in a real division when they played. The Power Score finds that real division for most of the games, not all (see OPEN below). The comment in Career that calls Hidden "administrative" holds the same wrong belief.
  - **Doug's data: 22 games against 8 teams that are Hidden now.** All 22 have a known real division. 13 come from the Power Score's lookup (view `v_power_score_team_matches_rated`); the step it used for each game was not recorded. 9 come from Doug's memory (they are NOT in the database; Zoo Pals' 2 may also be in the division history, UNKNOWN until the lookup runs live). Doug confirmed all 8 rows on 2026-11-16 and they are in `src/constants/dropoutDivisions.ts`.

    | Team (Hidden now)            | Real division | Games | Season                    | Source             |
    | ---------------------------- | ------------- | ----- | ------------------------- | ------------------ |
    | Burning Holes & Taking Souls | Recreational  | 6     | Summer 1 2026             | Power Score lookup |
    | Baggin' & Braggin'           | Competitive   | 4     | Spring 2026               | Power Score lookup |
    | Jerm                         | Recreational  | 2     | Winter 1 2026             | Power Score lookup |
    | Bag Assassins                | Intermediate  | 1     | Fall 2025                 | Power Score lookup |
    | Shut Your Cornhole           | Competitive   | 4     | Fall 2025                 | Doug               |
    | Zoo Pals                     | Intermediate  | 2     | Fall 2026 (active season) | Doug               |
    | Red Roof Rockets             | Recreational  | 1     | Winter 1 2026             | Doug               |
    | The Tomato Saucers           | Recreational  | 2     | Winter 2 2026             | Doug               |

  - **Behaviour before the Hidden fix (the same as before Slice 6; nothing changed in Slice 6):** the Team page counts a game against a Hidden team as a COMPETITIVE game (`calculations.ts:28`), which is the wrong tier for some (see the table: 14 of the 22 games were Recreational (11) or Intermediate (3); an earlier note said 13, which was the number of games the Power Score's lookup (view `v_power_score_team_matches_rated`) knew). Career drops all 22 (`calculateDivisionRecords.ts:27`). The Power Score rates the games it can resolve by the real division. It cannot rate 7 of them (INFERRED: Shut Your Cornhole 4, Red Roof Rockets 1, The Tomato Saucers 2); that is a separate scoring decision and was not touched.
  - **Kept from C1:** one test, in `processSeasonMatches.test.ts` ("reads the opponent division for the season being processed"). The same opponent is Competitive in season A and Intermediate in season B. A's game counts under Competitive only, B's under Intermediate only. Mutation-checked on a throwaway copy: a lookup fixed to season A, fixed to season B, or taking the first or last division of the opponent all fail it. Both the Team page and Career look the opponent up by team AND season (`teamDivisionMap.get(opponentId_seasonId)`); the Team page builds the map from `team_season_stats.division_name` (one row per team per season, `COALESCE(team_details_archive.divisionname, divisions.display_division)`, migration `20260818194322:107`). `calculations.ts` and its tests are byte-identical to the base commit `c4f69e7` again.
  - **BUILT 2026-11-16 (the Hidden fix, own commits on the same branch; it was OPEN): a dropped-out team's games count under its real division.** Doug chose: code only (no SQL, no migration, no new table); include the league card now; the Power Score lookup's 4 answers are right; and use the divisions he told us for the teams the lookup cannot answer. **Two sources, one non-fatal merge, applied where the four division maps are built.** (1) Doug's list, `src/constants/dropoutDivisions.ts`: 8 rows keyed "teamId_seasonId", all confirmed by Doug; it wins. (2) `fetchRealDivisionOverrides()` in `src/services/divisions/RealDivisionService.ts`: it reads `v_power_score_team_matches_rated` (the Power Score's own chain, migration `20261003183939`, lines 30-76) for every team-season that is Hidden now, so a team that drops out from now on needs no code when the snapshot or `team_division_history` can answer. The lookup never throws (a documented exception to "services throw": it only improves a display split, and the same fetch feeds career rankings and the admin sandbox): on an error it logs a warning and Doug's list alone applies. `applyRealDivisions` (`src/utils/divisions/realDivisionOverrides.ts`) sets an entry only when the stored name is missing, blank or Hidden, so a real stored name is never replaced. **Hook-ins:** Team page `fetchSeasonBreakdown.ts`; Career `CareerFetchService.ts` and `CareerBulkFetchService.ts` (one read for the whole run); league card `useLeagueDivisionMatchups.ts`. The active season (Zoo Pals) works because the map entry beats the joined current division in Career (`historicalDivision || fallbackDivision`). `categorizeDivision` (both copies) and every Hidden check are unchanged, as the fallback. **Result for the 22 games: Competitive 8, Intermediate 3, Recreational 11.** Team page: 14 games leave "Competitive" (3 to Intermediate, 11 to Recreational), 8 stay. Career and the Compare page: +22 games. League card: only games between two different tiers count. **Not changed:** season W-L, standings, Power Score, Career Power Score, playoff tiers, History pages. **Not done, a separate scoring decision:** the Power Score still cannot rate 7 of the 22 games (INFERRED). **No hand-apply.** Rollback: revert the commits. If an anonymous visitor cannot read the view, only a FUTURE dropout is affected (today's 22 games come from Doug's list); Doug's read-only anon check is planned before merge. The Career comment (`src/utils/career/calculateDivisionRecords.ts:21`) is fixed. Three other comments use the same word for LISTS (`src/utils/schedule/matchFilters.ts:29`, `src/lib/mcp/public/tools/_supabase.ts:99` and its twin `src/lib/mcp/tools/_supabase.ts:94`): hiding a dropped-out team from a list is right, so they stay.
  - **Optional later (not built):** (1) Share one in-flight lookup between the Team page breakdown and the career reads (5-minute TTL; cache only successes), so one page open does not read the view twice. (2) Read all `divisions` rows in parallel with the view read, so the chain is 2 reads deep, not 3. (3) Also filter the view by `season_id`, not only by `opponent_id`. (4) `pickRealDivisions` counts by division NAME, not by tier: 2 Competitive, 2 Competitive Low and 3 Intermediate would pick Intermediate, although 4 games say Competitive. (5) When a read fails, `handleDatabaseError` also calls `errorLog`, which sends an event to Sentry in production. That is a useful signal, but it can be noisy: one event on every page open while the read fails. (6) The live maximum rows setting that `fetchAllPages` relies on is UNKNOWN (`config.toml` says 1000). (7) **Doug's anon check** (the row count on `v_power_score_team_matches_rated` as a visitor, against the count as admin): the result goes here when he sends it. If the visitor sees fewer rows, only a FUTURE dropout is affected; today's 22 games come from Doug's list.
- **C2 (`7ac1c6f`): the Team page sweep rate leaves tied matches out of the bottom number**, like Career and Win % already do.
  - **What changed:** `calculateSweepRate` (`src/utils/teamDetailsUtils/sweepRateUtils.ts`) now needs a winner on a completed match, so the bottom number is wins plus losses. The field name `totalMatches` stays (only a test reads it). The comment in `leagueMatchStats.ts` now says "matches with a winner". The `winnerId` mapping keeps null or undefined for a match with no winner on all three feeds (`fetchRankingsData` through `transformDatabaseMatches`, `useTeamMatches`, `fetchRecapFacts`), and playoff matches always carry a winner, so the new filter cannot zero a rate.
  - **Example:** 8 completed matches, 4 wins (2 sweeps), 3 losses, 1 tie: 2/8 = 25.0% became 2/7 = 28.6%. A team with no tie does not change.
  - **Knock-on:** the same number feeds the Report Card "Offense" grade (1 of 9.5 weight) and the weekly recap grades. A team with a tie goes up, so other teams can shift one place. Published recaps keep the facts they stored. The Season Breakdown row was already tie-free, so the two numbers now agree.

The related files (`leagueMatchStats`, `TeamStatsComputed`, `StatBreakdown`, `gradeTeamsForWeek`, the report-card and compare tests) pass unchanged.

## Quality switch result (built 2026-11-17 on `claude/adoring-bohr-waxxok`; the one behaviour change in the PR)

**Answer first.** The "Prioritize Match Quality" switch now works in **Standard mode only**. Switch OFF is byte-for-byte what it was. Dual Match Mode is not changed. The first build failed the plan's gate ("ON bends no more rules than OFF") in some small blocks. Two independent QA reports found the cause. A 3-line fix (`preferFullRounds`, commit `a643717`) closes the gap in most cases. In 9 of the 12 tested cases ON found a good list in all 40 blocks (360 of 480 blocks). OFF found 268, 293 and 321 of the same 480 in the three library modes (reproduced in a separate check; not pinned by a test). In 2 cases no good list exists (3 Competitive, 1 Intermediate and 2 Recreational teams at 6 teams, and 5, 1 and 2 at 8 teams). In 1 case one exists but neither setting finds it (2 Competitive, 2 Intermediate and 2 Recreational teams at 6 teams). The tests use random ratings and 6 to 12 teams. Real block sizes are UNKNOWN. What is left is listed below under "What is left". Nothing here needs a hand-apply.

**Doug's answers (2026-11-17):** (1) Dual Match Mode: leave alone. (2) Division first. (3) Accept the amber note and the small shift in the quality rating. (4) Added later the same day, after the full-round fix: the red "N Warnings" badge and the score text. Doug chose **"Quiet badge, exact score"**: the amber note stays on neighbouring-division cards, the red badge counts only rematches and forbidden pairs, and a score under 5 never prints as 5.0 (see commit D below).

- **What was built (commits, one change each):**
  - `d25a84d` **C1, the score function.** `createQualityCompatibility` in `src/utils/autoSchedule/compatibilityUtils.ts`. Score = the division-only score (10 / 5 / 0) minus 0.5 x gap squared, rounded to the nearest 1/256 (1/1024 since 2026-11-17). The gap is a weighted average of four distances (power score over 100, strength of schedule, win rate, game win rate). A distance is skipped when either team has no data (power score null or 0; no decided matches). Same-tier stays in 9.5 to 10, next tier in 4.5 to 5, blocked stays 0. Equal data gives exactly 10 / 5 / 0.
  - `f3ddbfa` **C2, the wiring.** `scheduleStandardPairings` (`src/hooks/scheduling/utils/standardPairing.ts`). Switch ON means `config.weights !== undefined` (not `prioritizeQuality`). ON reads the real ratings of every paired team once, through `fetchTeamsByIds`, and keeps them in a Map inside the score function. The Team objects are not changed. OFF passes `calculateDivisionOnlyCompatibility` itself and reads nothing. If the read fails, Generate stops with a `BusinessLogicError`: "Could not load the team ratings that Prioritize Match Quality needs. Turn off Prioritize Match Quality and try again." Both screens show it in a toast: "Failed to generate match pairings: ..." (a plain database error would show only "Please try again").
  - `e17074d` **C3, the batch screen.** It now sends all four weights (5, 3, 3.5, 2). Before, it sent only 5 and 3.5. The values the Auto Schedule screen sends are unchanged.
  - `76e821c` **C4, text and comments.** Five screen spots (switch help text, "How Auto-scheduling Works" box, page header, batch footer, score notes under the preview) and three code comments now say: division comes first; with the switch ON in Standard mode a close power score and record are preferred next. They do not promise that divisions stay apart, and they do not use the word "guaranteed".
  - `c9db845` **Tidy, no behaviour change.** The failure message is one string, and three small gaps in the new tests are closed (a block with no list, a null win total, the counter the grid uses). It sits between C4 and C5, because the gate numbers in this section come from the tree after it.
  - `a4bc0ff` **C5, docs.** `SCHEDULER_README.md`, `docs/product-description/admin/build-the-schedule.md`, this section, the corrections-log row in `docs/agents/LEAGUE_CONTEXT.md`.
  - `a643717` **F1, the fix (3 changed lines of source).** `preferFullRounds?: boolean` added to the generator config (`blossom/types.ts`). The first run of `generatePairingsWithBlossom` is `generateOnce(teams, config, config.preferFullRounds === true)` (`blossom/generatePairings.ts`, was `false`). `standardPairing.ts` passes `preferFullRounds: config.weights !== undefined`, so only ON asks for it. The flag is not keyed on `weights` inside the generator, because Dual passes weights there too. **Rollback of the fix: delete the one line `preferFullRounds: config.weights !== undefined,` in `standardPairing.ts`.** (The flag in the generator then stays unused and false.) The tests that check the ON flag then go red: 45 on the tree of commit E (measured by deleting the line: 38 in `standardPairing.qualityGrid.test.ts`, 2 in `standardPairing.quality.test.ts`, 1 in `standardPairing.quality.realGenerator.test.ts`, 2 in `standardQualityRatings.integration.test.ts` and 2 in `ScheduleMatchesPreview.warnings.test.tsx`). Remove or update them. **ON's first pass now uses the vendored copy of the library (`blossom/vendor/`), like the retry already did.** ON is new, so no night that exists today changes. OFF is unchanged: it passes `false`, and the pinned tests stay green and unedited.
  - **This commit, words and numbers.** The overclaim "skill only decides between choices with the same division mix" is gone (code comments, README, product doc). The "N Warnings" note was added here and changed in commit D (next entry). The numbers below are final after commit E. `TESTING.md` counts are updated.
  - `14c1180` **D, the Warnings display (Doug: "Quiet badge, exact score").** `ScheduleMatchesPreview.tsx` counts a pair in the red "N Warnings" badge only when it scores under 4.5 or is a rematch. 4.5 is `LOWEST_NEIGHBOURING_DIVISION_SCORE` in `compatibilityUtils.ts` (5 minus the largest skill cut, 0.5; it is named from two new constants and no score changes). The amber card, icon and tooltip stay for every score under 5 (`MatchPairingItem.tsx`). `formatCompatibilityScore` prints a score under 5 cut to two decimals (4.99609375 reads 4.99; a trailing zero is dropped, so 4.5 and 0.0), and a score of 5 or more with one decimal, as before. OFF scores are only 0, 5 and 10, so OFF reads the same as before. Dual scores are 10 or 5 only, so Dual reads the same. **Rollback: revert the commit.** Tests: `ScheduleMatchesPreview.warnings.test.tsx` (16), `MatchPairingItem.score.test.tsx` (18), `compatibilityUtils.format.test.ts` (21), one added to `compatibilityUtils.quality.test.ts`, and two edited in `qualityWording.test.tsx`. Mutation check: with the constant set to 5, 8 tests go red; with `toFixed(1)` back in `MatchPairingItem.tsx`, 5 go red; with the preview back on `< 5`, 6 go red.
  - **E, words and docs.** Skill-term and division-first sentences made exact (code comments, README, product doc, this log); the seeded-grid claim reworded (the old text "in every block where a good list can be found, ON found one" was circular); the two cases where ON is not better written down; the Warnings text updated in all docs; one old line on the Auto Schedule help box fixed; `TESTING.md` counts updated.
- **The four weights are fixed numbers in code (5 / 3 / 3.5 / 2).** No screen edits them. They are not the power score weights (default 40/45/15; live 40/50/10 on 2026-11-17) and not the division weights.
- **Nothing is stored.** No SQL, no migration, nothing under `supabase/`, nothing to hand-apply. No power score, standing or history changes. Rollback: revert these commits (the docs too), or leave the switch OFF.
- **Accepted by Doug: the amber note.** With the switch ON, a pair from two neighbouring divisions whose teams are not close scores a little under 5 and shows "These teams may not be evenly matched" on the batch preview. Very close teams still score exactly 5 (**changed 2026-11-17, see "Finer rounding result" below:** a weighted gap under about 3.1% rounds to zero steps of 1/1024; when this was first built the step was 1/256 and the gap was about 6%, so 10 power score points with everything else level, a gap of 3.7%, changed nothing). **Accepted by Doug: the quality rating can shift.** A night that averaged exactly 8.0 with the switch OFF can average 7.97 with it ON (the Export tab still shows 8.0, but the rating is "Good", not "Excellent"). OFF is unchanged.
- **The red "N Warnings" badge counts only rematches and forbidden pairs (Doug's choice, commit D).** Before commit D it counted every pair that scored under 5, so with ON a clean night showed about 4 red warnings from neighbouring-division pairs that scored 4.5 to 5. Now `ScheduleMatchesPreview.tsx` counts a pair when it scores under 4.5 (that means 0: forbidden) or is a rematch. This is the same with ON, OFF and Dual: OFF scores are 10, 5 and 0, and Dual scores are 10 and 5. The amber card, info icon and tooltip "These teams may not be evenly matched" stay for every score under 5, and the card prints that score cut to two decimals (4.99, not 5.0). The preview text says so, and tests pin it.

The 14th (6 teams from mixed divisions) gives another list with the same one forced 0-score pair (see "Not built"); the test pins both lists; (d) every test that existed at `d2a8c7c` passes unedited (no such test file was edited).

The rule that a Dual night never calls the Blossom generator stays pinned by `standardQualityRatings.integration.test.ts`.**

### The acceptance gate: first result, cause, fix, final result

**First result (commit `a4bc0ff`, VERIFIED by running the seeded grid).** The plan said: "ON makes no more rule-bending pairs than OFF" (a rule bend is a Competitive team against a Recreational team, or a repeated pair unless both teams are Recreational). I built a seeded grid: tier mixes 1/2/1, 2/2/2 and 3/1/2 (Competitive / Intermediate / Recreational, the pattern repeated to the block size), blocks of 6, 8 and 10 teams, 40 seeded blocks each, random ratings. ON bent more rules than OFF in 4 of 9 cases in the plain library (for example 1/2/1 at 6 teams: 23 bends OFF, 25 ON; ON worse in 11 blocks) and in 7 of 9 cases in the all-throw browser mode. The grid counted only bends. It did not check that Apply can place a list into the two time slots, and it stopped at 10 teams. QA-B showed both gaps matter.

**Cause (VERIFIED by QA-A, QA-B and one experiment of mine; it is a twist on my first guess).** Take a block of 6 with 2 Competitive, 3 Intermediate and 1 Recreational team. A smaller round 2 (T1-T3 and T2-T5, 15 points) and the full round 2 (T1-T3, T2-T4 and T5-T6, also 15 points) tie in OFF. In ON every pair loses a little, so the round with fewer pairs loses less and wins. Both OFF and ON pick the short round. Then they split: in OFF the first run fails and the existing retry (full rounds) makes a clean list. In ON the two leftover teams are Competitive and the only open partner is the Recreational team. The relaxed repair step accepts that 0-score pair, nothing throws, so the retry does not run. A constant +0.5 on every score (my scratch experiment, not committed) also removed these bends, which fits the same cause.

**The fix (F1, commit `a643717`).** With ON, the first run asks each round to pair as many teams as it can (the library's `maxCardinality` switch, which the retry already uses). A smaller round can no longer win. See the commit list above for the 3 changed lines and the rollback.

**Final result (my seeded grid, VERIFIED by running it; commit `a643717`).** `standardPairing.qualityGrid.test.ts`: blocks of 6, 8, 10 and 12 teams, the same three tier mixes, 40 seeded blocks per case, in three library modes: plain; "real browser" (the library fails only when a round needs a blossom contraction, as a strict-mode browser does; 480 of 960 library calls failed in the run); and all-throw (the library fails on every call, as in `generatePairings.browserLibrary.test.ts`). A night is **good** when it is clean (no rule bent) AND placeable (`splitsIntoTwoSlots`: Apply can put it into the two time slots). Good nights, OFF to ON, out of 40 blocks per case:

| Case (mix and teams)          | plain          | real browser   | all-throw      |
| ----------------------------- | -------------- | -------------- | -------------- |
| 1/2/1 at 6                    | 17 to 40       | 35 to 40       | 35 to 40       |
| 1/2/1 at 8                    | 0 to 40        | 0 to 40        | 34 to 40       |
| 1/2/1 at 10                   | 11 to 40       | 40 to 40       | 40 to 40       |
| 1/2/1 at 12                   | 40 to 40       | 40 to 40       | 40 to 40       |
| 2/2/2 at 6                    | 0 to 0         | 0 to 0         | 0 to 0         |
| 2/2/2 at 8                    | 40 to 40       | 40 to 40       | 26 to 40       |
| 2/2/2 at 10 and 12            | 40 to 40       | 40 to 40       | 40 to 40       |
| 3/1/2 at 6                    | 0 to 0         | 0 to 0         | 0 to 0         |
| 3/1/2 at 8                    | 0 to 0         | 0 to 0         | 0 to 0         |
| 3/1/2 at 10                   | 40 to 40       | 25 to 40       | 33 to 40       |
| 3/1/2 at 12                   | 40 to 40       | 33 to 40       | 33 to 40       |
| **All 12 cases (480 blocks)** | **268 to 360** | **293 to 360** | **321 to 360** |

- **The gate that now holds (committed as tests):** in no seeded block is OFF good while ON is not (0 cases of 36); in every case ON has at least as many good nights as OFF; across the grid ON has more; and in 9 of the 12 cases ON finds a good list in all 40 blocks (360 of 480 blocks), in all three modes. (The OFF totals, 268, 293 and 321 of the same 480, were reproduced in a separate check; they are not pinned by a test.) In 2 cases no good list exists and in 1 case one exists but neither setting finds it. Mutation check: with the flag forced to false, 38 grid tests fail.
- **Bends (total over the blocks where both settings finished, OFF to ON), where they change:** plain: 1/2/1 at 6: 23 to 0, at 8: 80 to 0, at 10: 29 to 0; 3/1/2 at 8: 28 to 22. real browser: 1/2/1 at 6: 5 to 0, at 8: 80 to 0; 3/1/2 at 10: 15 to 0, at 12: 7 to 0; at 8: 23 to 17. all-throw: 1/2/1 at 6: 5 to 0, at 8: 6 to 0; 2/2/2 at 8: 14 to 0; 3/1/2 at 10 and 12: 7 to 0; at 8: 23 to 17. In the cases of 2/2/2 at 6 and 3/1/2 at 6 ON still bends more than OFF in some blocks (see below). These bend and stop numbers are the ones measured at 1/1024 (2026-11-17); a few moved by one from the 1/256 figures, see "Finer rounding result".
- **Stops (OFF to ON, blocks of 3/1/2 at 8 out of 40):** plain 11 to 1; real browser 18 to 1; all-throw 18 to 1 (at 1/256 each ON figure was 2). In one block of each mode (seed 4) ON stopped where OFF finished. No other case stops.

**Corrected claims (an earlier version of this section was wrong on two points).**

- "No clean schedule exists for 2/2/2 at 6" was **false**. A clean two-round schedule exists (round 1: C1-C2, I1-R1, I2-R2; round 2: C1-I1, C2-I2, R1-R2; QA-B brute force). Neither OFF nor ON finds it.
- "No clean schedule exists for 3/1/2 at 6 and at 8" is **true** under the no-repeat rule (the Recreational teams can only play the one Intermediate team or each other once, and an odd number of Competitive teams cannot be paired inside a round).
- The old text said OFF's bend count is the measure of quality. It is not: OFF's low bend counts at 3/1/2 sit beside lists that Apply cannot place. At 3/1/2 with 6 teams, 19 of OFF's 40 lists (plain library) cannot be placed, 33 in the other two modes; ON's: 6.

### What is left (VERIFIED by the grid, not fixed)

- **2/2/2 at 6 teams:** a clean schedule exists. Neither setting finds it. Both bend rules: 30 pairs OFF, 28 ON across 40 blocks (plain library; the test counts pairs, not blocks; at 1/256 it was 27 ON). Block by block the count goes both ways. At 1/1024 seed 36 bends one more rule under ON than at 1/256. In the all-throw library ON is worse than OFF in that one block (OFF 0, ON 1); in the plain and real-browser libraries both bend 1 there.
- **3/1/2 at 6 and at 8 teams:** no clean schedule exists, so a rule bends under both settings. Two things go against ON here. **At 6 teams (3 Competitive, 1 Intermediate and 2 Recreational) ON can bend a rule in more places than OFF:** 34 bent pairs ON against 21 OFF in the plain library, because OFF has 19 of 40 lists that Apply cannot place into the two time slots (ON has 6). **At 8 teams (5 Competitive, 1 Intermediate and 2 Recreational) ON stops less often than OFF overall (1 stop against 11 in the plain library), but in 1 of 40 blocks ON stopped where OFF finished:** the admin sees a red error toast.
- **How the grid is committed.** The old per-block gate "ON bends no more rules than OFF" runs as a plain test where it holds. For 2/2/2 at 6 and 3/1/2 at 6 it runs with `it.fails` and the true reason in a comment. The test "ON finds a clean, placeable list in every block" runs as `it.fails` for 2/2/2 at 6, 3/1/2 at 6 and 3/1/2 at 8, for the reasons above. When a later change fixes a case, its `it.fails` turns red on purpose and the entry becomes a plain test.
- **Numbers from QA-B (measured by QA-B in a throwaway copy, a bigger grid, NOT reproduced by me):** ON found a clean, placeable schedule in 600 of 600 blocks where one can be found, in all three library modes; OFF found one in 361 to 524 of the same 900. Before F1, ON at 1/2/1 with 12 teams was clean in only 14 of 60 blocks. My own grid gives the same direction (360 of 360 against 268 to 321 of 360) and 40 of 40 for ON at 1/2/1 with 12 teams. The sizes of the two grids differ, so the totals differ.

### Not built (on purpose)

- **A read-only check of `display_division`.** The pair score reads a tier through `getDisplayDivision` (an unknown name counts as Recreational) and the hard rules read it through `tierUtils.ts` (an unknown name counts as Intermediate). That mismatch is old (logged above, at the "A scoring question, not touched" bullet of the "Pairing retry result" section). It is why a block with a team named "Cuspers" or with no division can need one 0-score pair. Optional SQL for Doug: `select name, display_division from divisions order by name;` The answer is fine when every live division reads Competitive, Intermediate or Recreational.
- Making the old dead scorers (`calculateTeamCompatibility`, `calculateConfigurableCompatibility`) or `generateDualBlockPairings` do anything; a shared constants file for the four weights; merging ratings into the Team objects (it would change `analyzePowerScoreBalance`); an "n < 20" guard (for blocks of 20 or more teams the skill terms of one round could add up past one division step, which the code does not guard; the grid stops at 12; real block sizes are UNKNOWN); the integer-twin test. **Done in D4: all three were deleted (they did nothing and nothing called them).**
- **The plan's example scores** (AB 9.993 and so on) came with no ratings, so they cannot be reproduced. The committed A/B/C/D test uses four teams that are each one step stronger than the last (10 power score points, 0.1 of strength of schedule, 0.1 of win rate, 0.1 of game win rate). Its scores are now 10 - 5/1024 for one step, 10 - 20/1024 for two and 10 - 46/1024 for three (9.9951171875, 9.98046875, 9.955078125). They were 10 - 1/256, 10 - 5/256 and 10 - 12/256 (9.99609375, 9.98046875, 9.953125) until the 2026-11-17 finer rounding. Pairs ON: A-B, C-D, A-C, B-D. With the switch OFF the same four teams give A-D, B-C, A-C, B-D.
- **One block now differs from OFF with equal ratings:** 6 teams from mixed divisions (Competitive, Intermediate, Recreational, "Cuspers", "Recreational High", no division). Both lists hold the same one forced 0-score pair (A1-A4) and every team has 2 matches, but ON lists other pairs for the rest (full rounds). The test names this block and pins both lists. Every other block of the pinned file gives the same list with ON and equal ratings.

The step was 1/256: a Power Score gap of 10 points, with everything else level, changed nothing, and about 17 points were needed. Now about 8.4 points count. To go finer (1/4096 would make about 4.2 points count, QA-B: 5 points give 1 step) change the one constant `SKILL_STEPS_PER_UNIT` and the pinned numbers. ## Finer rounding result (2026-11-17, `claude/adoring-bohr-waxxok`; changes behaviour only with the quality switch ON)

**Answer first.** Doug chose a finer step for the quality switch: **1/1024** (it was 1/256). A Power Score gap of 10 points, with everything else level, now takes one step off the score. Before, it took nothing off. With the switch OFF nothing changes. Nothing is stored. No SQL. Nothing to hand-apply.

- **What changed.** `c9e31d6` (code): one named constant `SKILL_STEPS_PER_UNIT = 1024` in `src/utils/autoSchedule/compatibilityUtils.ts`, next to `MAX_SKILL_CUT`. The multiply and the divide in `createQualityCompatibility` both use it. The three "1/256" comments say 1/1024. `6ee183f` (tests): the tests that pinned 1/256 now pin 1/1024 (list below). This docs commit. Nothing else in `src`, `e2e` or `supabase/functions` held this step (searched for `256`: only unrelated SHA-256 and a 256-character limit).
- **The rule, exactly.** The cut is `round(0.5 x gap^2 x 1024) / 1024`. One step counts when `0.5 x gap^2 x 1024` is 0.5 or more, so when the weighted gap is **1/32 = 3.125% or more**. A gap of exactly 1/32 counts (a half rounds up). At 1/256 the line was 1/16 = 6.25%. The cut is never more than 0.5, which is 512 whole steps, so **the lowest neighbouring score is still exactly 4.5** and the bands stay: 9.5 to 10 for the same division, 4.5 to 5 for neighbouring divisions, 0 blocked.
- **What it means in Power Score points** (the screen weights 5 / 3 / 3.5 / 2 add to 13.5; only Power Score differs, the other three terms are level). The gap is `5 x (points / 100) / 13.5`. It reaches 1/32 at **8.4375 points**. Steps taken off, VERIFIED by running the real function: 5 points 0, 8 points 0, 9 points 1, **10 points 1**, 17 points 2, 30 points 6. At 1/256 the same gaps gave 0, 0, 0, 0, 1 and 2 (worked by hand: `0.5 x gap^2 x 256` is 0.04, 0.18, 0.51 and 1.58 for 5, 10, 17 and 30 points). A team with no decided matches has only the Power Score term: its gap is `points / 100`, so 3.125 points is the smallest that counts there.
- **Why 1/1024 and not 1/4096.** At 1/4096 a gap of 1/64 (1.56%) would count. With the screen weights that is about 4.2 Power Score points, and 5 points would take a step. So many more neighbouring pairs would show the amber note. 1/1024 is the smaller change in the amber note, and it fixes Doug's 10-point example.
- **The amber consequence (accepted by Doug on 2026-11-17).** With the switch ON, more neighbouring-division pairs score under 5 and show the amber note "may not be evenly matched": from about 8 Power Score points apart (before: about 17). The red "N Warnings" badge does not change: it counts only rematches and forbidden (0) pairs. A score under 5 still prints cut to two decimals. **5 - 1/1024 = 4.9990234375 prints "4.99", never "5.0"** (VERIFIED by an exhaustive check on a scratch copy: all 5,120 multiples of 1/1024 below 5 print as the exact value cut to two decimals, with a trailing zero dropped, and all 5,121 from 5 to 10 print with one decimal; 9.9990234375 prints "10.0"; `formatCompatibilityScore` needed no fix). The committed format test loops over every 1/1024 step below 5. The quality rating shift Doug accepted (an average of 8.0 can read lower, the rating can drop from Excellent to Good) is about the same size as before. Measured on the seeded grid (random wide ratings; the nights where both settings gave a clean list: 274, 301 and 331 in the three modes): the average pair score with ON was 0.029 to 0.030 lower than with OFF at 1/1024, and the same at 1/256 (single nights: 0.007 to 0.061 lower). So the example "8.0 can read 7.97" still fits wide ratings. A night with close ratings loses less. (QA suggested "about 7.99" for a typical night. The grid does not show that, so the examples were left as they are.)
- **Integer-twin test: not built, on purpose.** The matcher only adds, subtracts, doubles and halves the scores, so a power-of-two step keeps every sum exact. A test that runs the matcher on whole numbers would show the same thing.
- **Seeded grid (VERIFIED, run before and after the change).** `standardPairing.qualityGrid.test.ts` is green. **Every per-case "clean and placeable" count is the same at 1/256 and at 1/1024**, in all three library modes: ON 360 of 480 blocks in each mode; OFF 268 (plain), 293 (real browser) and 321 (all-throw). The per-case table under "Final result" above holds for the clean-and-placeable counts. **Bends and stops moved by one in two cases that are already marked as known gaps** (measured by QA, re-measured here on a scratch copy): 3/1/2 at 8 teams, ON stops 1 time in all three modes (was 2) and bends 22 in the plain library (was 23); 2/2/2 at 6 teams, ON bends 28 in the plain library (was 27) because seed 36 bends one more rule (in the all-throw library that makes ON worse than OFF in that one block: OFF 0, ON 1). "In 1 of 40 blocks ON stopped where OFF finished" is still true (seed 4, all three modes). All 15 `it.fails` cases stay expected-fail (none flipped). No gate was changed or loosened. (The counts come from a scratch copy of the grid that printed them. They are not pinned by a test.)
- **Tests changed (no `expect` line removed; the `calculateDivisionOnlyCompatibility` oracle assert in `compatibilityUtils.quality.test.ts` is untouched).**
  - `compatibilityUtils.quality.test.ts`: the 10-point test now expects `10 - 1/1024` (0.70 of a step). New: a 5-point and a 4-point gap are still exactly 10 (0.18 and 0.11 of a step); a gap of exactly 1/32 gives one step and 0.031 gives none; with the screen weights 8 points give 10 and 9 points give one step. The whole-steps check is now x1024 and also pins the constant at 1024. "Counts the four distances in proportion to their weights": 20 points give 2.81 steps, so 3 (`10 - 3/1024`). "Does not dilute the gap": 20.48 steps, so 20 (`10 - 20/1024`; the same number as the old `10 - 5/256`). The A/B/C/D ladder: gaps 0.1, 0.2 and 0.3 give `round(5.12) = 5`, `round(20.48) = 20` and `round(46.08) = 46` steps, so 9.9951171875, 9.98046875 and 9.955078125. Round 1 totals: A-B and C-D 19.9902, A-C and B-D 19.9609, A-D and B-C 19.9502, so the pairs are still A-B, C-D, A-C, B-D.
  - `compatibilityUtils.format.test.ts`: the loop over every step below 5 uses the exported constant; new rows 4.9990234375 ("4.99") and 9.9990234375 ("10.0").
  - `standardPairing.quality.realGenerator.test.ts`: the four pinned score strings (A|B and C|D 9.9951171875, A|C and B|D 9.98046875).
  - Checked and left alone (still green at 1/1024): `standardPairing.quality.test.ts` (50 against 52 points: gap 0.0074, 0.03 of a step, so exactly 10), `MatchPairingItem.score.test.tsx`, `ScheduleMatchesPreview.warnings.test.tsx` and the two `qualityWording.test.tsx` files. They use a given score number as input, and every 1/256 number is also a 1/1024 number.
- **Mutation check (VERIFIED, throwaway copy).** Step 256, 512, 2048 and 4096: 7, 7, 7 and 9 tests fail. Only the divide using 256: 10 fail. `Math.floor` instead of `Math.round`: 4 fail. `Math.ceil`: 6. `MAX_SKILL_CUT` 0.4: 8. Format cap 499 changed to 500: 1. Format rounding instead of cutting: 2.
- **How to go finer later.** Change `SKILL_STEPS_PER_UNIT` to another power of two (2048, 4096). Then update the pinned numbers (the titles and values in `compatibilityUtils.quality.test.ts`, the four strings in `standardPairing.quality.realGenerator.test.ts`, the docs) and re-run the grid. The format test follows the constant.

## Power Score check result (2026-11-17; Doug's read-only SQL on the live database)

**Answer first.** Doug ran read-only checks on the live database on 2026-11-17. **VERIFIED by Doug's queries; the developer did not re-run them.** Four things matter. (1) The live Power Score weights are **40 / 50 / 10**, not 40 / 45 / 15. (2) **Any "Save & rescore" in the Power Score Sandbox would change 62 stored team-season scores.** (3) **371 games are not rated** by the Power Score (364 in three old seasons, 7 in newer ones). (4) Doug wants accurate numbers, so the old-season fix is a separate job (design in progress). **Do not press "Save & rescore" until it is decided.** Nothing in the database or in the code was changed by this check.

1. **Live Power Score weights: 40 win / 50 strength of schedule / 10 game.** Applied 2026-09-03 (history row id 2). The baseline is 40 / 45 / 15 (id 1, 2026-08-20). So 40 / 45 / 15 is the **default** (what the migration seeded and what the code uses when the weights cannot be read). It is not the live setting. Docs that named 40 / 45 / 15 as the live weights now say "default" and give 40 / 50 / 10 as the live numbers.
2. **Division weights live:** Competitive 1.00; Competitive Low 0.95; cuspers 0.9 (shown as Intermediate); Intermediate High 0.8; Intermediate 0.7; Intermediate Low 0.60; Recreational High 0.6; Recreational 0.35; Hidden 0.6 (2 weight rows, the last on 2026-10-03); Hidden2 0.35. This matches the list from 2026-11-13 in `docs/agents/LEAGUE_CONTEXT.md`.
3. **Any Power Score weights save rewrites the stored scores of ALL seasons, from the live views.** A save with the same weights does it too (the screen then says "reasserted"), but the Save button is off when the typed numbers equal the live ones (`PowerScoreSandboxTab.tsx`, `canSave` needs `!unchanged`), so that case needs a stale tab or a second admin. The real danger is any save. Doug's check shows what a save would change right now: **62 team-seasons, all in Spring 2025 (20), Summer 1 2025 (21) and Summer 2 2025 (21).** Fall 2025 and every newer season: 0. Win-loss records: 0 changes, in every season. Division names: 0 changes, in every season. Largest moves: Here for Fireball +4.78 (Summer 2 2025, rank 30 to 28); Burning Holes & Taking Souls -4.54 (Summer 2 2025, rank 15 to 22); Double Trouble -3.86 (Summer 1 2025, rank 22 to 24); Mailmen +3.65 (Summer 2 2025, rank 23 to 17). **Likely cause (INFERRED, not checked):** migration `20261003183939` changed the opponent lookup and did not recompute those three seasons, so the stored scores are stale against the live views. **There is no table backup, and Revert works the scores out again from the live rules: it does not restore the old stored numbers.** Do not press "Save & rescore" until the old-season fix is decided. The false sentence in `docs/OPERATIONS.md` section 6b ("Saving the numbers that are already live changes nothing") was fixed. Two lines of Sandbox screen text were wrong in the same way, and were fixed afterwards (wording only, no change in behaviour; `src/components/admin/power-sandbox/PowerScoreSandboxTab.tsx`, tests added): the Save and Revert dialog said "It is fully reversible — the previous weights stay one Revert away" (now: "Revert goes back to the old weights. It works the scores out again. It does not bring back old stored numbers."), and the message for a save with the same weights said "Weights were already live ... every season was re-checked" (now: "Same weights. Scores rewritten." with "These weights were already live. The app still worked out all N stored season rows again, archived seasons included. Some scores may have changed.").
4. **Games the Power Score cannot rate** (view `v_power_score_team_matches_rated`, status "unresolved"):
   - **364 in the three old seasons:** Spring 2025 47 of 472 team-games; Summer 1 2025 169 of 558; Summer 2 2025 148 of 506.
   - **7 newer:** Fall 2025 x4 against Shut Your Cornhole; Winter 1 2026 x1 against Red Roof Rockets; Winter 2 2026 x2 against The Tomato Saucers. **All 7 are wins for the team that faced the dropped-out team.** If they were rated (Competitive 1.00; Recreational 0.35), 7 scores would move: Came from Dicks +1.69, Cuzzo's Clinic +0.65, Jager Bombers +1.22 and Pepperoni Cheesers +1.73 (Fall 2025); Buttery Nips -0.60 (Winter 1 2026) and -0.46 (Winter 2 2026); Happy Valley Hole Hunters -0.14 (Winter 2 2026).
   - **The stored per-season names of the 364 games' opponents** (the Power Score does not read names): Spring 2025 "Intermediate 1" (3 opponents, 47 games). Summer 1 2025 "Recreational" (5 opponents, 76 games), "Competitive" (4, 60), "Intermediate 1" (1, 17), "Intermediate 2" (1, 16). Summer 2 2025 "Competitive" (4, 58), "Intermediate 1" (3, 45), "Recreational" (2, 29), "Intermediate 2" (1, 16).
5. **The three oldest seasons also lean on guessed opponent divisions.** Of 472 / 558 / 506 team-games (Spring 2025 / Summer 1 2025 / Summer 2 2025), `current_division` (the division the opponent has today) resolved 121 / 292 / 282 games, and `last_known_division` resolved 31 / 80 / 58. These are the last two steps of the lookup, so many old-season games are rated by a guess.
6. **Doug's decision: "I want numbers to be accurate."** The fix for the old seasons (and for the 7 newer games) is a separate job. Design is in progress. **Nothing is applied without Doug's OK on a before-and-after table.** It will change numbers teams see. Until then: no "Save & rescore", and no change to stored scores. **Superseded 2026-11-18: the fix was built and applied; see "Job C result" and "Applied on the live database" below.**

Buttery Nips Summer 2 2025 65.6 to 51.9 (rank 9 to 22); Burning Holes & Taking Souls Summer 2 60.2 to 47.4; Mailmen Summer 2 54.7 to 66.0 (rank 23 to 10); Bag Babies Summer 2 56.8 to 66.3; The Great Cornholios Summer 1 45.4 to 33.7; Seize the Maize Summer 1 67.7 to 77.0.
- **Rounding finding (new).** On the live database `team_season_stats.power_score` is `numeric(8,3)` and `sos` is `numeric(6,3)` (from Doug's information_schema query); `career_power_score` is plain `numeric`. These limits are NOT in the repo migrations (live differs from the repo; CI keeps full precision). So the view can differ from a stored number, because storage rounds the view's number to 3 decimals (the two are equal only when the view's number has no more than 3 decimals): after each recompute the strict "still different" line showed 24, 25, 33 ... rows although the numbers were right (largest gap 0.000453 power, 0.000500 sos, 0.000000 career). The recompute writes about every row of a season (the rounded value). Archived seasons outside the six (Spring 2026, Summer 1 2026, Summer 2 2026) differ by up to 0.0005 for the same reason (Doug's L4 diagnostic query: power_score gaps 0.000465, 0.000500, 0.000482; sos gaps 0.000500, 0.000429, 0.000500).
- **Live-season drift.** Fall 2026 (the active season) had 27 to 28 team-seasons that differed from the view by up to about 0.06. Not caused by the fix: L2 passed (no game row outside the override rows changed) and override rows exist only in the six old seasons, so every input to Fall 2026 is unchanged. Its stored numbers refresh at the next scored match.
- **Fixed in the follow-up PR (this one): two scripts.** `division_truth_04_recompute.sql`: the last line is now "rows still different from the rules by more than rounding (must be 0)" (tolerance 0.0006 for `power_score` and `sos`; `career_power_score` exact). `division_truth_06_checks.sql`: check L4 uses the same tolerance, fails for a closed season outside the six, and counts the live season on its own line. The scripts test mimics the 3-decimal columns in a rolled-back savepoint (a stored gap over 0.0006 in a closed season fails L4; the same gap in the live season does not; the strict comparison, put back, turns the new cases red). **What "live season" means in L4:** the season `current_standings_season_id()` returns (the active season, else the playoffs-active season) AND not archived. A gap in an archived season, even one still marked active, is a FAIL. With no live season at all, a gap in any season outside the six is a FAIL. **Script 05 got the same fix in a later commit of this PR:** its report line that compares with the rules is now "... differ from the RULES NOW by more than rounding (0 after path 2)" (same tolerance); its line that compares with the backup stays exact (both are stored 3-decimal values); path 2's own recompute UPDATE is unchanged. The scripts test runs undo path 2 on the 3-decimal columns (the new line reads 0, the strict comparison finds 12 rows, a real gap of 0.01 is counted once).
- **Still true.** The Intermediate 1 / 2 order is believed, not proven, in all three old seasons. The backup table `team_season_stats_pre_division_truth` was kept until Doug accepted the new numbers (he did on 2026-11-18); script 07 retired it, **applied by Doug on 2026-11-19** (the table no longer exists; see "Backup retirement result" below); the undo scripts stay in the repo (only undo path 2 still works). A Sandbox save now keeps the fix. The section 6a Revert puts back the OLD formula (it does not read the override table): do not press it. The Sandbox weights Revert only goes back to the previous three weights and keeps the fix.

- **Backup retirement result (built 2026-11-18 on `claude/adoring-bohr-waxxok`; **APPLIED to the live database on 2026-11-19**).** **Answer first.** Doug accepted the new Power Scores on 2026-11-18 and asked to drop the backup table `team_season_stats_pre_division_truth`. Nothing was dropped by the code change itself.

Stored rows that differ from the rules by more than rounding: 0. Saved rows that differ from the stored numbers now (the accepted change): 107. Override rows: 86. Saved rows with no live row: 0. Saved rows whose record differs from the live row: 0. Override rows outside the six seasons: 0. Rated view has the override step: yes. Table exists now: no. Verdict: "OK: backup dropped". Do not run script 01 again.** Script 06 now skips L6b, L6c, L6d and L7 (INFO, "backup table not found").
  - **Four commits, in this order.** (1) Script 06 no longer needs the backup: its checks L6b, L6c, L6d and L7 read a temporary copy of it and say INFO, "skipped, backup table not found" when the copy is empty (L6a takes its team list from `team_season_stats`). Without this the whole of script 06 would fail at plan time once the table was gone. With the backup in place the output is the same as before (compared on the fixture, row by row; only the L5 fingerprint of `v_team_details` differs, and it differs between any two runs of the same script). (2) The new script 07: drops ONE table, no CASCADE, nothing else touched. (3) Text and one guard in the other scripts: script 03 STOPs when the backup table is missing (it needs the table); scripts 02 and 02a say the same, naming both reasons (first install: run script 01 before any override row; retired: do not recreate it; see "CodeRabbit review follow-ups" below) instead of the circular "run script 01 first"; the headers of 01 and 05 say what script 07 changes. The shared block of 02 and 02a is unchanged (md5 `ef4787e321419cdd417ef192463ca841` for both, before and after). No UPDATE or DELETE in any script changed. (4) The SQL test runs the real script 07.
  - **What script 07 does.** It takes the weight-save lock, prints pre-check lines (rows saved, when the backup was taken, saved rows per season, how many stored rows of the six seasons differ from the rules now by more than rounding (want 0), how many saved rows differ from the stored numbers now (the accepted change), saved rows with no live row (want 0), override rows (86 on 2026-11-18)), then drops the table. **Guard:** it STOPs, dropping nothing, if any stored score of the six seasons still differs from the rules by more than 0.0006 (the same test as check L4), or if a saved row has no live row in `team_season_stats` any more (its team-season was deleted since the backup), or if the override table has 0 rows: then the backup is the only way back or the only record. (More guards were added after review: see "CodeRabbit review follow-ups" below.) If the table is already gone it says "already gone" and changes nothing. There is no typed confirmation: the editor's own prompt for a destructive statement and the guard are enough.

Undo path 2 stays: it gives the numbers the OLD RULES give, not the old stored numbers. Doug must export the table as CSV before running 07 (required: it is the only way to keep the old stored numbers). The steps are in `docs/OPERATIONS.md` section 6g, "How Doug retires the backup".

After the drop, 06 runs, 03 and 02 STOP with their "retired" message, the dry run lists a CHECK row, undo path 1 STOPs and path 2 still reports. Section 9 of the test ends with a second real drop. The new tests turn red when: the stored-score guard is removed, the override guard is removed, the rounding test is made strict, the career comparison is removed from the guard, the DROP is removed, script 06 L7 reads the real table again, the script 03 guard is removed, or the old "run script 01 first" text comes back in 02a. All 37 SQL test files pass on a scratch Postgres 16 in one session, as CI runs them (Postgres 15 is not available here).

- **CodeRabbit review follow-ups (PR #1757; 3 commits: script 07 and its tests, the other scripts, docs).** Answer first: the guards of script 07 are stricter, the "backup missing" messages are honest, and the docs now say what the code does. Script 07 was then **applied by Doug on 2026-11-19** (see "Live result" above). The drop cannot be undone.
  - **Script 07, fixed.** (a) It takes the weight-save lock BEFORE it looks for the backup, so a run that had to wait reports "already gone" and not an error. (b) It then blocks writes to `team_season_stats` (share lock, reads stay open) until the drop, so no team-season can be deleted between the guards and the drop. (c) It STOPs if a saved row has a live row but a different record (wins, losses, game wins, game losses, division name, playoff rank, champion, runner-up: the same columns as "changed" in check L7); power, sos and career may differ (the accepted change). (d) It STOPs, before any score is compared, if the rated view has no `manual_override` marker (undo path 2 puts the old view back). (e) It STOPs if an override row is for a season outside the six (undo path 2 refuses to run then). New pre-check lines: saved rows whose record differs (want 0), rows for seasons outside the six (want 0), rated view has the override step (yes).

(g) Script 06 header: L7 owns saved rows whose live row is gone; L6b, L6c and L6d cover rows that still have a live row (logic not changed). (h) Scripts 02, 02a and 03 say the backup table is missing and name both reasons (first install: run script 01 BEFORE any override row; retired: do NOT recreate it), because they cannot tell which it is. The SHARED BLOCK of 02 and 02a is untouched (md5 `ef4787e321419cdd417ef192463ca841` in both). (i) Script 01 header: it stops only while override rows exist; after undo path 2 deletes them a rerun would make a NEW snapshot under the same name that cannot restore the original numbers, so never rerun it after the retirement.

(m) The CSV export is required before script 07, not optional. (n) `join-a-team.md`: the dropdown is cleared after a failed join too (the panel clears it after every attempt); the no-row case is a membership row that is gone or no longer editable by the user (the policy "Update membership" lets a user edit only their own row while it is not approved), and a change to a still-editable row does not block the overwrite. (o) `post-and-reply.md`: a message deleted in another tab makes the edit fail with the red toast, not report success.

Nothing was dropped. Run it again when nobody is entering scores." It also locks `power_score_division_override` in share mode (right after `team_season_stats`, before the first guard), so an override row cannot be deleted between the "0 rows" guard and the drop. This replaces the earlier "not locked" limit. The real timeout was checked by hand with a second connection that held a conflicting lock on `team_season_stats`, on the override table and on the advisory lock (each gave the STOP after 10 seconds). The single-session SQL test cannot open a second connection, so it checks the setting (`lock_timeout` = 10s after the run), both share locks (`pg_locks`) and the line order (timeout, advisory lock, both table locks, the timeout handler, then the backup test).

- **What was built.**
  - Migration `20261117130000_power_score_division_override.sql`: table `power_score_division_override` (team, season, division, reason; primary key team + season; division is `ON DELETE RESTRICT`, team and season `CASCADE`; row-level security on; public read; anon and signed-in users cannot write). The rated view `v_power_score_team_matches_rated` gets a new FIRST step, `resolved_by = 'manual_override'`, joined through `v_division_rateable`, so an override to a Hidden or weightless division is ignored and the 8 old steps run as before. Same 14 columns, same order, same types, `security_invoker = on` repeated. The body is the one from `20261003183939` plus two joins and two lines (diffed).
  - SQL tests `supabase/tests/power_score_division_override.sql` (built on a fixture that reaches all 8 old answers) and `supabase/tests/division_truth_scripts.sql` (runs the real script files; the dry run's rows equal the rows script 02 writes).
  - Scripts in `supabase/scripts/`: `division_truth_00_precheck.sql`, `_01_backup.sql`, `_02a_dryrun.sql`, `_02_data.sql`, `_03_preview.sql`, `_04_recompute.sql`, `_05_undo.sql`, `_06_checks.sql`, and later `_07_drop_backup.sql` (see "Backup retirement result"). Each ends in ONE result, because the SQL editor shows only the last result and does not show `RAISE NOTICE`.
- **Rows (script 02).** One per opponent team-season of the three old seasons that has at least one game rated by `division_history`, `current_division`, `last_known_division` or `unresolved` (a team-season answered by a snapshot, the archive or a bracket gets none; `manual_override` is not in the list, so a re-run adds 0). The division comes from the team's stored season name through Doug's map, matched on `lower(btrim(divisions.name))`, never on `display_division`. The estimate was about 85 rows; the live run wrote 86 (83 team-seasons and the 3 explicit rows). Doug's exception wins: "Here for Fireball" is Recreational High in Summer 1 and Summer 2 2025 (reason "League owner, 2026-11-17: was Recreational High"; no reason text names a person, because the table can be read through the public API). Three explicit rows for the 7 newer games. The script stops if a mapped name matches other than one rateable division, if "Here for Fireball" matches other than one team, if the backup or the migration is missing. An unmapped stored name inserts nothing and is listed.
- **Recompute (script 04).** One season per run, only the six seasons (the three old ones, Fall 2025, Winter 1 2026, Winter 2 2026). It takes the Sandbox weight-save lock and runs one narrow UPDATE of `power_score`, `career_power_score`, `sos` and `recorded_at` where the stored value differs (`IS DISTINCT FROM`) from `v_team_season_agg`. The same statement as `admin_recompute_season_power`, without the admin check (the SQL editor has no signed-in admin). It is NOT `upsert_team_season_stats(true)` and NOT the Sandbox save.
- **Preview (script 03).** A one-time step: once the backup is gone it STOPs at once with a plain message. Three numbers per team: **stored** (the site today), **live_rules** (today's rules, from the backup's `live_*` columns taken before any override), **after**. So the 62 old team-seasons that already differ (older rule changes never recomputed; the tie backup table was NOT FOUND live) are separated from this fix. It also shows the change, the part caused by this fix, the rank before and after, and how many games changed weight (worked out against the old view text, which the script saves as a temporary view). **Career rows (added in the fix round):** for each team whose career score changes, the career score before, under today's rules, and after, plus the career rank. It reuses the real formula (`calculate_career_power_score()` in `20261117120000`, lines 150-345, and `calculateCareerPowerScore.ts`, lines 100-140): the match-weighted average of the stored season scores over ALL of a team's seasons plus the live season, with the six seasons swapped. The bonus does not change with this fix; it is read from the real function (career today minus the base worked out from stored numbers). A team capped at 100 today is marked `*` and its live_rules and after numbers are lower limits. Checked on a scratch copy: the predicted career score after equals the real function after the recompute for every team (15 of 15 listed; none outside the list changed).
- **Undo (script 05).** Path 1 puts the saved numbers back (today's stored numbers, exactly; safe because the six seasons are closed). Path 2 first copies the override rows to `power_score_division_override_pre_undo` (so a row added by hand is not lost), then deletes the rows, puts the view of `20261003183939` back and recomputes: that gives the numbers today's rules give, not today's stored numbers. Path 2 then path 1 goes all the way back. Tested on a scratch copy: the stored table and the view fingerprints were identical to the start.
- **Checks (script 06).** L1 to L8 from the plan (L1 solid answers unchanged, L2 rows and records unchanged, L3 override game count and no unrated game left in the six seasons, L4 drift only in the six seasons, L5 live-season views read no override game, L6 scores re-derived with the live formula, L7 records identical to the backup, L8 TypeScript list = SQL rows; L3b every override game's weight equals the division's weight today). Once script 07 has dropped the backup, L6b, L6c, L6d and L7 say INFO, "skipped, backup table not found"; the rest run as before. **L9 cannot run:** the three old seasons have no playoff brackets.
- **Not built, on purpose.** No TypeScript change (`resolved_by` is `string | null`; `RealDivisionService` skips only `'unresolved'`), no `types.ts` change (auto-generated), no admin screen, no Sandbox change, and `src/constants/dropoutDivisions.ts` is NOT deleted (it decides the Team page; the SQL row decides the Power Score; L8 compares them). No name step in the view, no back-fill of `team_division_history`, no change to the archive, no numbers-only patch (the Skeptic rejected all four).
- **Honest limits.** More correct, not perfect. (1) A game is rated by the weight on the game date; the weight history starts 2026-08-12; for the six map divisions Doug's live read (2026-11-17) shows one history row each, so today's weight applies. (2) One division per team per season. (3) Old playoff games use a best-effort date for the weight. (4) The stored name is a label and the map is Doug's decision: "Intermediate 1 = Intermediate High" and "Intermediate 2 = Intermediate Low" are believed, not proven, in ALL THREE old seasons, because those seasons have no brackets. (5) The six seasons are archived and have 0 regular games (VERIFIED by Doug's live read, 2026-11-17). If a season were un-archived or held a regular game, the live standings (`v_team_details`) could move for that team when the rows are written, not when the season is recomputed. Script 00 and check L5 show it, so re-run script 00 right before step 3. (6) If a label hides the wrong division, a team moves by about 0.2 for each game it faced against a Competitive Low team mixed up with Competitive, and by about 1 for each game it faced and won against a Recreational High team mixed up with Recreational (INFERRED, by hand).
- **Live facts Doug read on 2026-11-17 (VERIFIED by his paste; they settle the points the first build left UNKNOWN).** The editor role is `postgres` (bypasses row-level security, owns the rated view). The view has `{security_invoker=on}` and the `division_history` step. `team_division_history`, `division_archive_distrust` and `v_division_rateable` exist. All six seasons have `is_archived = true` and 0 regular games. Each of the six map divisions has exactly one weight-history row (Competitive 1.00, Recreational 0.35, Intermediate High 0.80, Intermediate Low 0.60, Intermediate 0.70, Recreational High 0.60). Every old team-season has a stored name in the map. These checks must be re-run right before step 3 (script 00): a season could be un-archived.
- **Plan corrections found while building (VERIFIED in code).** (a) There is no `supabase/README.md`. The hand-apply order is a table in `docs/OPERATIONS.md` section 6g, and the new test is listed in `supabase/tests/README.md`. (b) The §6a Revert and Re-apply buttons do NOT rebuild `v_power_score_team_matches_rated` (the saved text in `20260812171710` rebuilds `v_team_details`, `v_team_match_stats` and `v_team_season_agg` only). So they cannot drop the override step. But Revert puts back the old formula, which never reads the override table, and then rewrites every stored season with it. That is the real warning, and it is in `docs/OPERATIONS.md` sections 6a and 6g.
- **Tests (scratch Postgres 16; CI uses 15).** All 37 SQL test files pass in one session the way CI runs them, and one by one (37 of 37 again after the polish round). The new test fails under each of 21 mutations of the migration (a missing grant or policy or row-level security, a write grant for anon, the Hidden join, the delete rule, NOT NULL, `security_invoker`, the override step in the wrong place, a join without the season or on the wrong team, an inner join, a dropped old step, a changed label or weight order). Two of them survived the first version of the test and led to the extra fixture rows for every old step and for a re-weighted division. **Known CI blind spot (QA found it by mutation):** CI Postgres has no Supabase default privileges, so a deleted `REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated` line in the migration cannot be caught: on Supabase that line removes the default write grants, and on CI there are none to remove. The test asserts the grants the migration writes, but not that the default ones are gone. Read that line in review. No fake test was added. The empty-table fingerprint of the view (256 rows on the fixture) is identical before and after the migration, and so are `v_team_season_agg`, `v_power_score_components`, `v_power_score_components_current` and `v_team_details`. Each script was run on a fixture of the real season ids and the three real dropped-out teams, including the stop cases and a second run.
- **Docs changed.** `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (section 6g; warnings in 6a and 6b), `docs/agents/LEAGUE_CONTEXT.md` (three rows), `docs/product-description/stats/power-score.md`, `src/utils/powerScore/README.md` (the `resolved_by` table), `supabase/tests/README.md`, this log.
- **Review round (three reviews of the first build; all approve with changes; no change to the view logic was needed). Summary as the Lead gave it to me; the full review texts are not in the repo.**
  - **QA, round 1:** approve with changes (PARTIAL). **Main finding: there was no pre-check before step 3.** Check L5 ran only after the override rows were written. The BEFORE step 3 box and script 00 (precheck) were added for this. It also found that an empty `reason` was accepted although the test said it cannot be, and one mutation that CI cannot catch (a deleted `REVOKE` line). One minor point: the Sandbox weights Revert also rewrites every stored season.
  - **QA, round 2:** PARTIAL again, nothing broken. The 37 SQL test files passed on Postgres 16 and on a real Postgres 15.20 (37 of 37; as the Lead reported it; I could run only 16 here). It asked for wording fixes and one script fix, all done in the polish round: the dry run (02a) now says "script 02 would STOP" for a missing dropped-out team or season, the scripts test turns JIT off (44 s to 0.8 s), the undo header says the restore recipe puts back rows only, and the docs say exactly what Doug's read showed.
  - **supabase-data:** approve with changes. Script 02 wrote first and printed afterwards (a dry run was asked for); the `reason` text is publicly readable, so it must hold no person's name; an undo should not lose a row added by hand.
  - **competition-scoring:** approve with changes. The weight wording ("today's weights") was too strong; no effect-size cap may be stated; the preview should show the career score too; a check should compare each override game's weight with the division's weight.
  - **What the fix round changed (3 commits on top of the first build).** Commit A: `CHECK (btrim(reason) <> '')` on the table, a test for an empty and a spaces-only reason, and the CI blind spot written down (tests README and the section above). Commit B: script 00 (precheck), script 02a (dry run, one shared block of SQL with script 02), the career part of script 03 (reuses the career formula; see its header), check L3b and a plain next step in L3, `reason` texts that say "League owner" (not a name), undo path 2 saves the override rows first, script 04 can run twice in one transaction, and the new SQL test `supabase/tests/division_truth_scripts.sql`. Commit C: the runbook box "BEFORE step 3", the live facts above, the weight wording, the warnings for the Sandbox Revert and for hand-written rows, and this section. **Polish round (2 more commits):** the dry-run CHECK rows for a missing team or season (with a test that script 02 really stops), `jit = off` in the scripts test, the undo header sentence, the "What the editor may ask" note, the four places that read the view before step 6, and exact wording for what the live read showed.
  - **Bot review of PR #1750 (batch 3; each point checked against the code before any change).** Fixed: undo path 2 now STOPS, writing nothing, if an override row belongs to a season outside the six (it recomputes only the six); its copy and delete of the override rows is one statement; its report no longer fails when the backup table was dropped; script 00 also requires `is_active = false` and `playoffs_active = false` (`current_standings_season_id()` follows those flags and ignores `is_archived`), and Doug's live read did not include those two columns, so script 00 must be re-run right before step 3; the numeric assertions in the override test now fail on a NULL (the old `IF abs(...) > tol` passed when a value was NULL); script 01's verdict compares keys and saved scores, not only row counts; the 6b sentence about the 6a Re-apply is exact (migration `20260820184423` patched it); the help page keeps only the game-date rule in the main text. **Declined, with reason:** check L1 stays strict (a team-season is all strong or all weak, because the snapshot, archive and bracket steps depend on the opponent and the season, never on the game; so if an override ever replaces a strong answer, L1 is supposed to FAIL), and `games_affected` in the shared block stays (the number is the same for the same reason).

The plan said the section 6a Revert and Re-apply buttons rebuild the rated view and would drop the override step: they do not (see "Plan corrections" above); the real danger is the old formula that never reads the override table.

The real module is `@/hooks/useSeasonalTheme` (it exports `useSeasonalThemeBase`). Fixed. All 9 tests pass with the mock now active (checked on a throwaway copy: a throwing mock fails all 9).
