## Holes PR G result (a stale phone cannot end a game someone reopened), built 2026-11-20 on `claude/adoring-bohr-waxxok`; 2 commits; the first has no migration, **the second (undo and round edits) is a migration and must be hand-applied**

Answer first: **ending a game now refuses a phone whose screen is out of date. Before, a stale phone that still showed "game won" could end a game an admin had just reopened, and silently overwrite the correction.** Doug decided on 2026-11-19: refuse it and ask the scorer to check the screen (option B). No migration. No SQL test. No scoring change.

- **What changed (VERIFIED by reading the code and running the tests; the database behaviour is INFERRED from how PostgREST works, nothing was run against a real database).**
  - `LiveMatchService.completeGame` (`src/services/liveScoring/LiveMatchService.ts`) takes a fourth argument, `seenUpdatedAt` (required): the raw `games.updated_at` string the screen last loaded. An empty string throws `ValidationError` before any database call, so a future caller cannot drop the guard by accident.
  - The update now carries `.eq('id')`, `.eq('status', 'in_progress')` and `.eq('updated_at', seenUpdatedAt)`, then `.select('id').maybeSingle()`. The `games` table has a trigger that sets `updated_at` on every update (`set_games_updated_at`, `20260709120308_...sql:75-78`), so a reopen makes the old value stale.
  - **The raw string is never parsed.** It is not passed through `Date`, `toISOString` or `Date.parse`. Postgres keeps microseconds and JavaScript dates cut them, which would make the guard refuse every finish.
  - If the guarded update matches no row, the game is read again (`GAME_COLUMNS`, never `select('*')`) and the answer is chosen from what is stored now.
  - `useGameFlow.ts`: `CompleteGameInput.seenUpdatedAt` (required), passed as the 4th argument. `ActiveGamePanel.tsx`: the End game click passes `game.game.updated_at`, read at click time inside the existing `onConfirm`. The mutation's `onSettled` already refetches the game, so the screen updates after a refusal (nothing added).
- **The 5-way decision (only when the guarded update matched no row).**

| What the re-read finds                                                                                                                         | Result                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| No row                                                                                                                                         | `BusinessLogicError`: "This game no longer exists. Refresh the page."                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Completed, same winner AND stored totals equal the totals we tried to write                                                                    | **Success, nothing shown.** Covers a double tap, a second scorer ending the same result, a lost reply, a match already finalized. Compared with `Number()` against the INPUT values, never the row's own. A stored null never counts as 0. `completed_at` and `updated_at` are not compared. |
| Completed, different winner or totals                                                                                                          | "Someone else already ended this game with a different score. Check the score. Reopen the game if it is wrong."                                                                                                                                                                              |
| In progress, `updated_at` differs from the one seen                                                                                            | "This game was changed by someone else. The screen is updated. Check the score, then end the game again if it is right."                                                                                                                                                                     |
| In progress, `updated_at` equals the one seen (the guard did not fail, so something else blocked the write: row-level security or lost access) | The old message: "Nothing was saved. This match may already be finished, or you can no longer score it. Refresh the page."                                                                                                                                                                   |

A re-read database error goes through `handleLiveScoringError(..., 'Failed to check the game')`.

- **Why a status check alone does not close the gap.** `reopenGame` sets the game back to `in_progress`. A stale phone's write would then match `status = 'in_progress'` and overwrite the correction. The `updated_at` match is what makes the reopen visible to the old screen.
- **Tests.**
  - `LiveMatchService.test.ts`: the `completeGame` block now uses the chain `update -> eq -> eq -> eq -> select -> maybeSingle` and a second chain `select -> eq -> maybeSingle` for the re-read. Fixtures use a microsecond string (`2026-11-20T01:23:45.123456+00:00`) and one test asserts that exact raw string reaches `.eq('updated_at', ...)`. The old success and database-error tests stay (the success test keeps every old assertion); the old "0 rows" test became the "in progress, same updated_at" test. New: the three guard filters; no re-read on success; no re-read on a database error; empty version gives `ValidationError` and no database call; re-read uses explicit columns and the id; completed with the same winner and totals resolves (also with numeric strings); a different winner refuses; same winner with different totals refuses; stored null totals are not read as 0-0; in progress with a newer `updated_at` refuses; row missing; re-read database error; re-read missing table gives `LiveScoringNotEnabledError`.
  - `useGameFlow.test.tsx`: the exact 4-argument call, and the failing-signal test passes the new field.
  - `ActiveGamePanel.test.tsx`: new test: confirming the win sends `seenUpdatedAt` equal to the game's raw `updated_at`.
  - `LiveMatchView.test.tsx`: the game fixture got a real `updated_at`, and the two exact-call assertions include `seenUpdatedAt`. `useLiveMatchRealtime.reopen.test.tsx` only mocks the service and needed no change.
  - **Mutation checks (each went red, then green after the file was restored byte for byte).** Drop the `updated_at` filter: the filter test goes red (1). Let null count as 0: the null-totals test goes red (1). Drop the winner comparison: the different-winner test goes red (1).
- **What this means on league night.** If an admin reopens Game 2 to fix a score and a scorer's phone still shows the old "game won" banner, that scorer's End game press is now refused with a red message and the screen updates. Before, it silently ended the game again with the old totals. A double tap, or two scorers ending the same result, still just works.
- **NOT covered (on purpose; recorded here so nobody assumes it is).**
  - **CLOSED later in the same PR (2026-11-20, second commit): undone or edited rounds now move `games.updated_at`.** See "Undo and round edits (database trigger)" below. The first commit left this open; the text above describes the first commit.
  - `reopenGame` and `setGameWinner` are **not guarded**. A reopen or an admin correction must always work.
  - `RoundService`, undo and round edits are untouched in TypeScript.
- **Docs.** `docs/product-description/live-scoring/finish-a-game.md` (Submit and the End Game edge case), `docs/product-description/verification/live-scoring.md` (new row `GAMEEND-25`, needs two devices; nothing marked verified), one sentence in `docs/product-description/bug-triage.md` (the `completeGame` note). The "Unfixed rule gap" line of the Holes PR A list and the "STILL OPEN" line of PR B are marked done. `docs/agents/LEAGUE_CONTEXT.md` has a new corrections-log row.
- **NOT changed.** `types.ts`, `supabase/`, scoring, tie-breaks, winner rules, `reopenGame`, `setGameWinner`, the round services. No history impact.
- **Counts.** 14 tests added (13 in `LiveMatchService.test.ts`, 1 in `ActiveGamePanel.test.tsx`), no files added or removed. CI coverage run (`CI=true VITEST_DEEPSOURCE=1 VITEST_CI_COVERAGE=1 VITEST_FAST_COVERAGE=1 npx vitest run --coverage`): exit 0, 891 files, 8,545 passed plus 15 expected-fail (8,560 total; was 8,546). `TESTING.md` now says 891 files / 8,560 tests in both places. `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint` and `npm run knip` were clean.
- **Rollback.** Revert the commit. Nothing is stored.

### Undo and round edits (database trigger), second commit of Holes PR G

Answer first: **undoing a round, or an admin editing or deleting one, now makes a stale phone refuse to end the game.** Doug asked for this in the same PR. **It is a migration, so it must be hand-applied** (`docs/OPERATIONS.md` section 6).

- **What changed (VERIFIED: the migration and its SQL test ran on Postgres 16.15, the whole CI chain plus all 38 smoke files, and the new test is mutation-checked).**
  - New migration `supabase/migrations/20261120120000_touch_game_on_round_change.sql`: function `public.touch_game_on_round_change()` and trigger `touch_game_on_round_change` (AFTER, row level) on `public.match_rounds`, firing on `DELETE` and on `UPDATE OF game_id, team1_score, team2_score`. The function touches the game only on a `DELETE`, or on an `UPDATE` where `team1_score`, `team2_score` or `game_id` really differs from the old row.
  - It sets `games.updated_at = now()` on the round's game, only when that game has `status = 'in_progress'`. An update that moves a round to another game touches both games. `completeGame` is not changed: its `.eq('updated_at', seenUpdatedAt)` filter now matches no row, so the existing refusal message shows.
  - `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path = pg_catalog, public` (the CI check `security_definer_search_path.sql` needs the pin, and a scorer's RLS must not block the touch). `EXECUTE` is revoked from PUBLIC, anon and authenticated. No FORCE RLS exists, so the owner bypasses RLS.
- **Why DELETE and UPDATE only, no INSERT (supabase-data and security-reliability reviews).** A round is inserted on every round. A touch there would make the scorer's OWN screen look stale right after their own round (false refusals) and add a realtime refetch for every round. A stale phone never needs it: rounds cannot be added after the win.
- **Why UPDATE is limited to those columns, and why the function compares old and new (QA finding, fixed in the same commit).** They are the ones the totals depend on (`net_points` and `winner_team` are generated from the two scores). QA found that `UPDATE OF <columns>` fires whenever a column is in the SET list, even with the same value, and the admin Edit Round dialog (`EditRoundDialog.tsx`, `buildRoundUpdate`) always sends both scores, so a thrower-only or bag-only admin save also moved the game and caused one needless "changed by someone else" refusal. The function now returns early on an `UPDATE` where both scores and `game_id` equal the old row (the check is in the function, not a `WHEN` clause, because a `WHEN` that reads `NEW`/`OLD` cannot be shared with the `DELETE` event). A thrower or bag-count edit, with or without the same scores re-sent, does not move the game.
- **Why only in-progress games.** A completed game is history. The trigger never writes one. (`reopenGame` already moves `updated_at` through `set_games_updated_at`.)
- **Safe on cascades (tested).** Deleting a game or a match removes its rounds; the trigger finds the game gone or in the same delete and updates no row. `validate_games_row` only checks `winner_team_id`, which is NULL on an in-progress game, so the touch cannot raise. Nothing on `games` writes back to `match_rounds`.
- **Residual window, NOT closed on purpose.** `fetchLiveMatchBundle` reads matches, games and rounds in parallel. If the rounds read lands before a committed undo and the games read lands after it, a stale phone can still pass the guard. The window is milliseconds and the realtime event refreshes the screen. Closing it would add a request round trip to every refresh.
- **Test.** `supabase/tests/touch_game_on_round_change.sql` (new, line added to `supabase/tests/README.md`). It plants an old `updated_at` (2000-01-01), because `now()` never changes inside one test transaction, and resets it between steps with `session_replication_role = replica`. It checks: the function, the pinned search path, no EXECUTE for anon or signed-in, and the trigger shape (AFTER, row, DELETE and UPDATE, not INSERT, the three columns); a delete moves its in-progress game while a control in-progress game and a completed game keep the old value; a score edit moves it (both team columns); a thrower-only edit does not, in both forms (score columns left out of the SET list, and the same scores sent again as the admin dialog does); moving a round to another game moves both in-progress games, and moving it into a completed game moves only the old one; an insert does not; a scorer (approved team member, not admin) undoing the last round under `SET LOCAL ROLE authenticated` moves it; **the real proof:** the guard's own statement (`UPDATE games SET status = 'completed' ... WHERE status = 'in_progress' AND updated_at = <seen>`) matches 0 rows after an undo and after an admin score edit, and 1 row on an untouched game (so the zeros are not a guard that never matches); a round change on a completed game leaves it alone; deleting a game and deleting a match with an in-progress game and 2 rounds raise nothing and leave no rows.
- **What this means on league night.** A scorer undoes the winning round on their phone while a second phone still shows "game won". The second phone's End game press now gets the red message "This game was changed by someone else..." and its screen updates. Before, it ended the game on the old totals.
- **Docs.** `docs/OPERATIONS.md` section 6 (hand-apply note), `docs/product-description/live-scoring/finish-a-game.md`, `docs/product-description/verification/live-scoring.md` (new row `GAMEEND-26`; GAMEEND-25 note), one sentence in `docs/product-description/bug-triage.md`, the Holes PR G row of `docs/agents/LEAGUE_CONTEXT.md`.
- **NOT changed.** App code, `types.ts` (no table or column changed), scoring, tie-breaks, `completeGame`, hooks, realtime. No history impact: nothing is written when the migration runs, and completed games are never touched afterwards. No vitest test changed, so the counts in `TESTING.md` stay.
- **Hand-apply.** Paste the migration file in the Supabase SQL Editor and run it (idempotent, safe to paste twice). Run it when no game is being scored: it takes a brief lock on `match_rounds` (`DROP TRIGGER` needs an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock for a moment). The editor may show a "destructive operation" prompt because the file has `DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS`; that is expected, click through. Verify with the query in `docs/OPERATIONS.md` section 6, BEFORE (0 rows) and AFTER (1 row on `match_rounds`, enabled `O`, definer true, fixed path `search_path=pg_catalog, public`, neither `anon` nor signed-in users can run it, definition `AFTER DELETE OR UPDATE OF game_id, team1_score, team2_score ... FOR EACH ROW`). Until it is applied, the old gap stays open (nothing else breaks).
- **Rollback.** `DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS touch_game_on_round_change ON public.match_rounds;` then `DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS public.touch_game_on_round_change();`. Nothing stored to restore.

## Live-scoring audit trail result (built 2026-11-20 on `claude/adoring-bohr-waxxok`; 2 commits: migration + SQL test, docs; **a migration, it must be hand-applied**)

Answer first: **the database now records who undid or edited a round, reopened a game, changed a winner or deleted a game, and when. Scorers are recorded too. There is no screen: Doug reads it with SQL.** Before, nothing recorded any of it (`games` and `match_rounds` were not on the admin audit list, and that audit skips every non-admin). Doug decided on 2026-11-20 to **record scorer actions too, not only admins** (an undo by a scorer is the correction nobody else can see). No app code changed, `types.ts` unchanged, no scoring change, no history rewritten (nothing is written when the migration runs, no back-fill).

- **The final rule, in plain words.** For any signed-in person, one row goes into `security_audit_log` (existing table, existing writer `log_security_operation`, so `user_id` is the person, `created_at` the time, `old_values` the whole row before, `new_values` the whole row after, NULL for a delete):
  - `live_round_delete`: a round was deleted (scorer undo, admin delete).
  - `live_round_update`: a round really changed (score, thrower, bag count). A save that re-sends the same values writes nothing (the admin Edit Round dialog always re-sends both scores).
  - `live_game_change`: a game that was **completed** is changed (a reopen by a scorer or an admin; an admin's winner or score change).
  - `live_game_delete`: a game was deleted.
- **What is NOT logged.** INSERTs (a round goes in all night). The normal finish (`in_progress` to `completed`). The touch trigger's `updated_at` write (`20261120120000`; it only writes in-progress games). A caller with no `auth.uid()` (cron, service role, SQL editor). Cascaded rows: a match delete (2 games, 3 rounds) writes **0 rows**; a direct game delete writes **one** `live_game_delete` row, not one per round. A match delete itself is not logged (`matches` has no audit trigger).
- **Why scorers are included (Doug, 2026-11-20).** The old admin audit (`audit_admin_mutation`) returns early for a non-admin. The scorer undo ("Scorers delete last round" policy) is the most common correction on league night, and it is the one nobody could trace. `current_user_is_admin()` is **not** used. `audit_admin_mutation()` and `admin_audit_coverage_drift()` were **not touched** (shared by about 17 tables).
- **Design (VERIFIED: three reviews agreed, then built and run).** `public.audit_live_scoring_correction()` is `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path = pg_catalog, public` (a scorer cannot write the log table; CI `security_definer_search_path.sql` needs the pin), `EXECUTE` revoked from PUBLIC, anon and authenticated. Three triggers, all AFTER and row level: `audit_live_scoring_correction` on `match_rounds` (UPDATE or DELETE; the function decides whether the row really changed), `audit_live_scoring_correction_upd` on `games` (UPDATE, with `WHEN (OLD.status = 'completed')`) and `audit_live_scoring_correction_del` on `games` (DELETE). The games triggers are two because a `WHEN` clause cannot read `NEW` on a delete. Own drift helper `public.live_scoring_audit_coverage_drift()` (no rows = fine): checks the function is `SECURITY DEFINER`, and each trigger exists, is enabled (`O`), row level, AFTER, has exactly the right events (no INSERT, no TRUNCATE) and the games UPDATE trigger has its `WHEN`.
- **Failure-safe (tested).** The whole function body is one block with `EXCEPTION WHEN others THEN RAISE WARNING ...; RETURN NULL;`. If the audit insert fails, the scorer's undo or Doug's edit still lands, and the database prints a warning. **Scoring never fails because of the audit.** The test puts `CHECK (false)` on `security_audit_log` inside a savepoint and shows an admin round edit and a scorer undo still succeed with 0 audit rows (and that the direct audit write really is refused, so the zero is not a trigger that never ran). The cost: if the audit fails, that one correction is not recorded (the warning is the only trace).
- **Cascade finding (VERIFIED, with evidence).** Deleting a game or a match cascades to `match_rounds` (and a match to `games`), and every cascaded row fires the AFTER trigger. **The skip works:** inside the function, a round is skipped when its game or its match no longer exists, and a game when its match no longer exists. In an AFTER trigger fired by the foreign-key delete, the parent row is already gone. Proof is in the test (a match with 2 games and 3 rounds deleted by an admin, and the same through `delete_match_with_stats_reversal`: 0 `live_*` rows). **Mutation:** taking the round skip out makes the match delete write 3 rows; taking the game skip out makes it write 2. So both halves matter and are pinned.
- **Tests.** New `supabase/tests/live_scoring_audit_triggers.sql` (added to `supabase/tests/README.md`; the README also now lists `admin_audit_triggers.sql`, which it had missed). It runs with the real roles (`SET LOCAL ROLE authenticated`, claims through `auth.set_test_claims`) and counts as the owner, because scorers cannot read the log. It asserts: the function (definer, pinned path, no EXECUTE for anon or signed-in users) and the three trigger shapes; the drift helper is clean, and it flags a dropped trigger, a disabled trigger, a trigger widened to INSERT, a games UPDATE trigger without its `WHEN`, and a missing function (4 rows); an admin score edit writes one `live_round_update` row naming the admin with both snapshots; re-sending the same scores writes 0, a thrower-only change writes 1, an `updated_at`-only change of a completed game writes 0; an admin round delete writes one `live_round_delete` row with the whole old row and the touch trigger's games write is not logged; a **scorer** undo succeeds and writes one row naming the scorer; a scorer reopen of a completed game succeeds and writes one `live_game_change` row (completed to in progress); an admin winner change writes one more; **normal flow writes 0** (scorer and admin round inserts, a scorer finishing a game from in progress, an update of an in-progress game); no `auth.uid()` writes 0; the failure-safe case above; the two cascade cases; an admin game delete writes exactly one `live_game_delete` row and none for its rounds. Inside one transaction `now()` never moves, so old `updated_at` values are planted under `session_replication_role = replica`, as in the touch test. The log shows two expected `WARNING: audit_live_scoring_correction failed ...` lines (case 9).
- **Local run (VERIFIED on a throwaway Postgres 16.15, UTF8; CI uses 15).** Bootstrap plus all migrations as `supabase-ci.yml` does, then the new migration a second time (idempotent: no error, same 3 triggers), then all **39** smoke files in one `psql` run, exit 0 (the new one, `admin_audit_triggers.sql` and `security_definer_search_path.sql` among them). **Mutation checks (each applied after dropping the function and triggers, so old grants cannot hide anything; each made the new test fail; the migration was restored afterwards and the test passed):** add INSERT to the `match_rounds` trigger, add INSERT to the `games` DELETE trigger, remove the EXCEPTION block, remove the `auth.uid()` NULL guard, drop the `WHEN` on the games UPDATE trigger, drop the no-real-change guard on `match_rounds` UPDATE, drop the no-real-change guard on the games change, `SECURITY INVOKER`, remove the REVOKE, make it admin-only (a scorer undo then writes 0), remove the round cascade skip, remove the game cascade skip. 12 of 12 killed.
- **What this means on league night.** Nothing changes for scorers or admins: no new button, no new message, no slower undo. If a score is disputed later ("who removed round 3?"), Doug runs the report in `docs/OPERATIONS.md` section 6h and sees who, when, and the deleted round's scores (a copy he can put back by hand).
- **Hand-apply (required).** A merged migration does not reach production. Paste the whole file in the Supabase SQL Editor at a quiet time (no game being scored; it takes a brief lock). Optional first line `SET lock_timeout = '5s';`. The "destructive operation" prompt is expected (`DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS`). Run the BEFORE check (0 rows), the file, then the AFTER check (3 rows, all `O`), `SELECT * FROM live_scoring_audit_coverage_drift();` (no rows), and a read of the newest `security_audit_log` rows. The real proof is the first genuine correction: a scorer undo on a throwaway match gives one `live_round_delete` row, and finishing a game gives none. All steps and queries are in `docs/OPERATIONS.md` section 6h. **Until it is applied, nothing is recorded and nothing else breaks.**
- **Rollback.** Five `DROP` lines at the end of the migration header (three triggers, two functions). Rows already written stay in `security_audit_log`.
- **Honest gaps (on purpose, or not worth the cost).**
  - **No screen.** SQL only. (A screen would be a product decision for later.)
  - **A change in the SQL Editor or by the service role is not logged** (no `auth.uid()`).
  - **An admin changing the winner of a game that is still in progress is not logged.** `setGameWinner` on an in-progress game looks the same as a normal finish.
  - **Match-level actions are not audited**: reopen a match (`reopen_live_match`), save it as finished, delete it. `matches` has no audit trigger today. A match delete shows only as missing rounds and games.
  - **If the audit write itself fails, that correction is not recorded** (a warning is the only trace). That is the price of "scoring never fails because of the audit".
  - **A direct game delete does not keep its rounds.** Its `old_values` holds the game row only. (A direct round delete keeps the whole round.)
  - **Live is UNKNOWN until the hand-apply:** whether the older admin audit has ever written a row on the live database was not checked; the "read the newest log rows" step in section 6h shows it.
  - `LIVEFIX-24` in `verification/admin.md` is **not** marked verified: its Result stays "—" until Doug runs the post-apply check.
- **Docs.** `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (new section 6h), `docs/product-description/admin/correct-a-live-match.md` (the "Nothing records that a correction happened" edge case, rewritten), `docs/product-description/live-scoring/correct-a-round.md` (one edge case), `docs/product-description/verification/admin.md` (`LIVEFIX-24` rewritten, `LIVEFIX-10` one clause), `docs/product-description/verification/live-scoring.md` (the audit-trail open question closed with a pointer; `FIX-20` reworded to "no screen"). The older "no audit trail" notes in this file and `bug-triage.md` are left as dated history; the one in "Holes PR C5 result" is marked closed with a pointer.
- **Small stale doc lines fixed in the same commit (VERIFIED in code).** Two docs said the Challonge fallback "Remove" button deletes a saved bracket with no confirmation. The code asks first: `ChallongeFallbackSection.tsx` has a `ConfirmDialog` titled "Remove this saved bracket?" (Cancel and Remove), pinned by `ChallongeFallbackSection.test.tsx` ("deletes an existing bracket via deleteBracket, after confirming"). Fixed in `docs/product-description/admin/run-the-playoffs.md` (two places) and `docs/product-description/admin/site-settings.md` (three places), and in the two verification rows that tested the old claim (`PLAYADMIN-21`, `SETTINGS-14` in `verification/admin.md`; Result still "—"). A new, never-saved row still drops from the form with no prompt (it stores nothing).
- **NOT changed.** App code, `types.ts`, `audit_admin_mutation()`, `admin_audit_coverage_drift()`, the touch trigger, scoring, tie-breaks, RLS policies. No history impact. No vitest test changed, so the counts in `TESTING.md` stay.

## Carry-over notes for later slices

- **Follow-ups from the tests-only DeepSource PR (2026-11-13):** the "Looks wrong, NOT fixed here" list (6 items, 2 need Doug first: scheduling skip in item 1, head-to-head weighting in item 4) and the uncovered dead-code lines are recorded in the section "Tests-only PR for the DeepSource coverage lines" above.

- **DONE 2026-11-11 (commit 0db935f): `updated_at` on legacy playoff writes.** `uncoveredPlayoffHooks.test.tsx` now checks the ISO `updated_at` that `usePlayoffMatchUpdate.ts:170` stamps. Mutation-checked (deleting the line fails the test). `updatePlayoffMatchResult` can now be removed whenever a later slice wants. (Removed in D3b.)
- **DONE 2026-11-11 (commit ed9e86b): `sos || 0.5` fallback** (`hooks/useTeamRankings.ts:88`, display only). Two cases in `useTeamRankings.test.ts`: a missing SOS reads 0.5; a real 0.62 stays 0.62. Mutation-checked. A SOS of exactly 0 is not tested on purpose (league rules hold SOS between 0.1 and 1.0).
- **DONE 2026-11-11 (commits 210c8a6, a69d29d): head-to-head counts.** New `supabase/tests/head_to_head_records.sql` covers `get_head_to_head_records` (view `v_head_to_head`, used by `HeadToHeadService`) and `get_batch_head_to_head` (used by `TeamCareerStatsService` / schedule page). Pins: matches + playoffs + archive all count; tied and unfinished regular-season/archive matches do not; mirrored rows; (B,C) zero row; self pair dropped. QA recomputed every number by hand and mutation-checked the view and the batch function (archive, playoff and `iscompleted` branches, game-win sides, tie handling). Ran on Postgres 16.15 only: CI uses 15 (see open items below).
- **Head-to-head notes (found while writing the head-to-head test). The first is SETTLED in Slice 5; the other three stay open for Slice 6:**
  - **SETTLED in Slice 5: a repeated pair in the weekly recap is NOT a number-changing bug.** `get_batch_head_to_head` squares its counts when the same ordered pair is listed twice (twice = x4, three times = x9). `useBatchHeadToHead` removes duplicate pairs first, so the schedule page is safe. `weeklyRecap/upsets.ts` (~line 125) builds `pairs` from the week's completed matches with NO de-duplication. That is harmless: a repeated ordered pair means two decided matches of those teams are in the week, so both are already counted and the total is already past `MIN_H2H_MATCHES` (2); multiplying every count by the same number leaves wins / (wins + losses) unchanged (`predictionRatings.ts:54-56`); and the upset check reads only `probA` / `probB` (`upsets.ts:168`). **Kept as a documented SQL quirk. No fix needed.** Pinned by `predictMatch.test.ts` ("head-to-head counts that are all multiplied", mutation-checked).
    - **One narrow edge, VERIFIED by reading, NOT seen in data, NOT pinned:** a tie is counted by neither head-to-head function (header of `supabase/tests/head_to_head_records.sql`). If one of the two same-order matches in the week is a tie and the teams have no earlier meeting, the true total is 1 (below the floor of 2) and x4 reads 4, so the head-to-head term switches on for that one recap row. It needs the same two teams, in the same order, twice in one week, and one of the two a tie. Recommendation: leave it. De-duplicate `pairs` in `upsets.ts` only if it is ever seen.
  - **The two functions disagree on one edge case.** A completed match with a winner but a NULL `loser_id` is dropped by the view (`get_head_to_head_records`) and counted by the batch function. Not pinned (looks accidental). Check production data before deciding. **PINNED in Slice 6** (Doug's check found 0 such rows live; behaviour left as it is).
  - **Playoff status is not filtered by either function.** A playoff match with a winner and loser set counts even if its status is `in_progress`. Described in the test header, not pinned. **PINNED in Slice 6** (Doug's check found 8 such rows, all Fall 2025, each with a winner and a score; both functions count them; left as is).
  - **Postgres 15 vs 16.** CI runs Postgres 15; the new test ran only on 16. The view returns `numeric` game wins (playoff scores are numeric) while `get_head_to_head_records` declares `bigint`. Postgres 16 casts silently; 15 is expected to as well (the function already exists in the migrations CI applies on 15). If the first CI run fails on this function with "return type mismatch", that is a real bug that needs a migration, not a test change.
- **CI fix, Deno edge-function tests (2026-11-11, PR 1736):** the job `Edge function Deno tests` failed at random with `Integrity check failed` for `esm.sh/tslib@2.8.1` and `esm.sh/zod@3.23.8`. Cause: `esm.sh` rebuilt its files (origin `last-modified` 2026-10-21; some CDN edges still hold Nov 2025 copies), so the same unpinned URL returns old or new bytes depending on the edge, and `deno.lock` can only hold one hash. The two versions differ only by redundant parentheses (cosmetic). Fixes made: `deno.lock` refreshed to the origin hashes (commit 6ba80be; all 63 entries re-verified), and `--no-lock` added to the CI test command in `.github/workflows/supabase-ci.yml` (Doug approved "fix properly in CI"). Reproduced locally with Deno 2.9.6: lock enforced fails with the CI error, `--no-lock` gives 148 passed. The stale workflow comment that said "no deno.lock is committed" was corrected.
  - **Left open (production hardening, not done):** edge functions import from `esm.sh` unpinned (`zod@3.23.8`, `@anthropic-ai/sdk@0.71.0`, and `@supabase/supabase-js@2`, a floating major version). A deploy therefore bundles whatever `esm.sh` serves that day. Pinning exact versions plus a build version (`?pin=v135`) would make deploys repeatable, but it changes what production runs (the repo test lock resolves `supabase-js` to 2.112.4; production may differ), needs a hand-deployed test, and I cannot deploy from here. Do this as its own change with a deploy plan.
  - `deno.lock` is now unused in CI (auto-found only because `package.json` sits next to it). Decide later whether to keep or delete it.
  - Not code problems, seen the same day: GitHub runner could not resolve Ubuntu mirror names during `playwright install --with-deps` (browser check); Cloudflare Workers Build failed on a corrupted `npm` cache (`ENOENT .../_cacache/...`); retry or clear the build cache.
- **Playoff match tie:** live code gives team 2 the win when game wins are tied (`usePlayoffMatchUpdate.ts:154`, `useOptimisticScoreMutation.ts:250`). The screens cannot reach it (quick editor offers 2-0, 2-1, 1-2, 0-2). No test pins it. Ask Doug only if a different rule is wanted.
- **DONE in Slice 7 (2026-11-17): stale comments removed** (they named deleted callers): `TimeslotQueryService.ts:44,103`, `BracketInfoService.ts:91`, `BracketMatchReadService.ts:12`, `MatchHistoryService.ts:105` (fixed in design PR D3), `src/docs/FOLDER_CONVENTIONS.md:76`. The functions they describe may now have no production caller: check with `knip --production`.

Refresh it with `npm run test:coverage:update-baseline` (it runs the coverage gate, replaces `coverage-baseline.txt`, and syncs the baseline table in `TESTING.md`). `npm run test:coverage:refresh-docs` does NOT write `coverage-baseline.txt`: it only syncs `TESTING.md`.
